DOCUMENT: Series_Spines_and_Treatments/The_Runaway_Escape_Arc_Master_Bible.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Story Bible
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (Screenplay / Novella Outline | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Dilemma:** The internal collision between societal safety (yielding to father, boyfriend, and corporate career) versus radical, messy self-exploration.
>
> **Consolidated Character Arcs:**
> - **Act I (The Breaking Point & Juhi Dialogue):** The suffocating office, the superficial "coaching badminton" career advice, and impulsive flight to Himachal.
> - **Act II (The Boyfriend Resolution):** Deconstructing love beyond codependency or completion; returning ready to apologize only to discover he has cleanly moved on.
> - **Act III (The Boss Confrontation):** Running into Sanjay in an airport lounge en route to Delhi; refusing corporate pleasantries to demand honesty about empty promotion promises.
> - **Act IV (The Father Resolution):** Confronting the patriarch; recognizing that obedience guarantees neither happiness nor immunity from failure.

---

## Act I: The Inciting Dilemma & The Juhi Dialogue

The dilemma at the heart of the story is the battle between what the world expects and what you want to become—especially when you don’t yet know who you are.

She thinks her problem is her job, her relationship, and the relentless pressure from her father. The surface dilemma: stick it out, say yes to the boyfriend and father, and live an expected, safe life—or just run away to make the noise stop.

### Scene: Coffee with Juhi
> **HER:** What if I just go somewhere?  
> **JUHI:** Go somewhere? You want to go on a trip?  
> **HER:** No. Just go. Quit my job, and just run away without telling anyone.  
> **JUHI:** *(Laughs)* Sure. Where will you go?  
> **HER:** I don't know. The hills. Maybe Himachal.  
> **JUHI:** You know I have a friend... She lives in Shimla. She likes it there. But she has to walk all the way to the store to get groceries. Amazon delivers in 10 days. You don't even get Uber there. What will you do?  
> **HER:** I'll write. Travel. I don't know.  
> **JUHI:** Why do you want to go? You have a good job. A steady relationship. It's more than what most have.  
> **HER:** Leave it.  
> **JUHI:** Tell me.  
> **HER:** No, leave it.  
> **JUHI:** Arey tell me yaar.  
> **HER:** I hate my job. I hate it I hate it I hate it.  
> **JUHI:** Change it. Take another job. It's fine.  
> **HER:** It'll be the same. I don't feel like...  
> **JUHI:** Feel like what?  
> **HER:** I don't know how to explain it. I hate working in an office. I hate the work I'm doing, I hate answering to my—  
> **JUHI:** DO something you like then.  
> **HER:** What?  
> **JUHI:** Do something you like! You hate the work here, right? So just think: what do you want to do, and do it.  
> **HER:** Mmmm-hmmm.  
> **JUHI:** Like, what do you like doing?  
> **HER:** Many things man. Like... playing badminton.  
> **JUHI:** Okay! Write a badminton blog. Coach young kids.  
> **HER:** What are you talking about?  
> **JUHI:** Yeah. Do what you like!  
> **HER:** Do you hear yourself? How idiotic you sound. Just start coaching? What do I even know about coaching? I like playing badminton, I don't want to do it all my life. What the fuck, Juhi.  
> **JUHI:** I was just...  
> **HER:** So. Just run away.

---

## Act II: The Boyfriend Arc (Love vs. Moving On)

### Thematic Focus: Defining Love
She initially thinks the relationship is tying her down because she is stuck in her own skin. She assumed love meant "completing" each other, but that makes romance purely transactional. 

True love is enhancing: recognizing someone deeply, understanding their flaws and strengths, accepting them, and supporting them without keeping a ledger.

### The Climax Beat
She returns after months in the mountains, finally grounded in herself. She expects a dramatic reckoning where she either asks for him back or delivers a noble, tearful goodbye.

Instead, when she meets him, he listens to her apology calmly and reveals that he has simply moved on. He has been dating someone else for two months. 

The realization stings, but there is no breakdown. She realizes she had a chance at love, lost it through her own avoidance, but is fundamentally intact. She doesn't need him to rescue her; she can take care of herself.

---

## Act III: The Boss Confrontation (Airport Lounge)

### Scene: Running into Sanjay
En route to Delhi, killing time in the airport lounge with free food and a book, she sits down directly across from Sanjay, her former boss.

> **SANJAY:** Hello? What happened to you? Where were you? You just vanished.  
> **HER:** Hi. I was doing some soul searching.  
> **SANJAY:** *(Laughs)* Whose soul were you searching? Anyway, how've you been? Where are you working now?  
> **HER:** Nowhere.  
> **SANJAY:** Really? I thought you left to join a competitor.  
> **HER:** No. I ran away. Spent time in Himachal and Uttarakhand. Now I'm writing.  
> **SANJAY:** Writing. That's... great.  
> **HER:** Sanjay, tell me something. When you told me we’d do big things next year—that I’d get the promotion and the pay raise—did you actually mean it? Or was it just the standard script to keep me billing forty extra hours a week?  
> **SANJAY:** Of course I meant it. We have a business to run—  
> **HER:** I know you have a business to run. But could you try being honest with people? Just once. Without the corporate lecture.

She walks to her gate without anger. He was never an evil mastermind—just another small-time manipulator exploiting convenience.

---

## Act IV: The Father Resolution (Revoking the Approval Trap)

### Thematic Focus: Breaking Conditional Control
Her prolonged inaction in life was not her father's fault; it was her own cowardice in seeking his permission. She used his authoritarian expectations (arranged marriage pressure, corporate career tracks) as an excuse to avoid taking responsibility for her own choices.

### The Resolution Beat
She confronts him not with fury or adolescent rebellion, but with grounded gratitude and a clean boundary:

> *"I love you, and I will always remember how you took care of me—taking me to school, helping me with homework, traveling together. I will always want your advice. But you have to let go.
> 
> My life belongs to me now. If I end up broke, disgraced, or miserable, they have to be my mistakes. Because even if I do everything you say, you cannot guarantee my success or my happiness. Only I have to live with the outcome."*

She leaves him without needing an apology or an endorsement.

---

## Act 0.5: Roshan's Silence & The Fake NGO Applications

### The Withheld "I Love You"
The friction with Roshan (the boyfriend) isn't loud or explosive; it's a cold, prolonged starvation. 
In two full years of dating, she has never said *"I love you."* 
She assumed her silence was a gentle buffer, signaling the relationship's natural limits. Instead, he mistook her emotional distance for modest reserve, using it to rationalize stepping past her to ask her father for permission to marry.

### The Application Cover
To justify her impending disappearance, she fabricates experience on international NGO fellowship applications in public development—listing volunteer work for an uncle's trust. The application advice says *"be true to yourself,"* and she cynically calculates: *"I've put in enough palatable bullshit to make them think I'm exactly who they want."*

---

## Subplot Option: The Bumbling Extortion Scheme (The Boss & The Colleague)

In a darker, satirical variant of the narrative arc:
When she vanishes from the office without submitting a formal resignation, her manager (facing payroll shortfalls) and a desperate subordinate uncover where she went through a mutual friend. 

Rather than reporting her absence responsibly, they concoct a half-baked scheme to call her authoritarian father and demand ransom, pretending she has been abducted before she reaches Himachal. The plan collapses immediately when her father contacts the police, leading to their arrest in an absurd, botched payoff.

---

## Act 0.2: The Promotion Trap (Ramesh and the Creative Director Bait)

### Scene: Ramesh's Office
Somya attempts to claim the career trajectory promised during her recruitment two years ago.

> **SOMYA:** Hi Ramesh, can I talk to you for a moment?  
> **RAMESH:** Sure, I have five minutes.  
> **SOMYA:** So... I wanted to talk to you about my role. You know I'm an advertising major, and I came to this agency because I was promised a creative position if I did good work.  
> *(Ramesh stares at her in silence.)*  
> **SOMYA:** Since you said you were happy with my work, I was hoping I could interview for the open Creative Director position.  
> **RAMESH:** That's a good idea. Ganesh already has a lot of workload and could use help. Are you ready for it? Good is fine, Somya. I need great. Do you commit to being great?  
> **SOMYA:** Yes, sir. I will be.  
> **RAMESH:** Most important thing is never to disappoint the client. So what we'll do is: talk to Ganesh, let him offload some of his grunt work to you. We'll check your progress on a schedule and see if you can handle the responsibility.  
> **SOMYA:** And... the title?  
> **RAMESH:** You have to complete your existing assignments on time as well. I'm not going to hand out a position without testing you. Would you do that if you ran this company?  
> **SOMYA:** No...  
> **RAMESH:** Exactly. Talk to Ganesh, finish your existing load, and we'll review your progress.  
> **SOMYA:** In how much time, sir?  
> **RAMESH:** About a year. That sounds fair. If you can't take the responsibility, it's fine. We can have this conversation again when you're ready.  
> **SOMYA:** No... sir. I'm ready.  
> **RAMESH:** Very well then.

---

## Protagonist Interiority: Somya's Dual Deceptions

### The Secret She Won't Tell Herself
She convinces herself that escaping to Himachal or landing an overseas fellowship will solve her life[cite: 68]. In reality, the fellowship is an academic hiding spot—a two-year shield to dodge a breakup and freeze her father's marriage demands without having to confront either[cite: 68]. Her partner is attentive and loyal; her guilt stems from the fact that she simply doesn't love him, yet lacks the courage to hurt him[cite: 68].

### The Secret She Won't Tell Anyone Else
At 32, societal panic is closing in[cite: 69]. She dodges arranged marriage biodatas from her father, but secretly fears that walking away from a stable relationship leaves her unmarriageable in the eyes of her family and peers[cite: 69]. She stays silent not out of contentment, but out of fear that she lacks the skills to survive outside prescribed corporate tracks[cite: 69].

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/Giraffe_Designs_and_the_Ten_Foot_Escape.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Sharmita tiptoeing past Mr. Achyutan's cabin at Giraffe Designs LLC; freezing in ballet poses when someone sneezes; getting saddled with a 3-day Mahishasur pitch brief; escaping outside to face a buzzing phone with 7 unread messages from dad
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - Link Sharmita's escape to [[The_Runaway_Escape_Arc_Master_Bible]]
> - Polish the sensory tension of tiptoeing past the boss's cabin

---

On its best days, Giraffe designs llc was an afterthought for local businesses in Bangalore, on its worst it was their first choice. Giraffe was a pretentious company that believed in its own ideas. The name Girrafe was a personal insight from Mr Achyutan “...it signifies the ability to look over barriers.” he used to tell clients. He was proud of that analogy. He thought it perfectly described what he wanted his company to do. Look over barriers.

Sharmita peaked from her cubicle. She wanted to see if Mr Achyutan was still there. He was. She sat back down, clicked a minimised window and started going through her facebook feed. She stopped at a picture.
Taking a break from work! Exploring life!!!!  #awayfromreallife #chillscenes #wanderlust
She stopped reading after wanderlust. She hated that word. It seemed everybody had this lust to wander and they were ready to flaunt it.
She peaked again. Mr Achyutan was on a call. “He might not notice” she thought. She walked towards the photocopier pretending she had to pick up papers. The copy machine was next to Mr Achyutan’s cabin. She could walk to the copy machine and sneak past the cabin if she was careful.

She picked up a couple of pages from the table on which the machine whirred and whizzed all day long.

Mahishasur Mantri Masala marketing pitch

STRATEGIES TO INCREASE PAGEVIEWS
• Search engine optimization
• Paid ads
• Social media marketing
• Viral content...

“Same old shit!” she looked inside the cabin.
“Oh shit! Did he just see me?” she asked herself. “Should I go back to my cubicle?”. She was unsure. Mr Achyutan was still on a call and he might have glanced in her direction. “Doesn’t matter. I was anyway trying to get a few papers from the copy machine” She told herself. “Even if he saw me, I was doing my job.”

She was now facing her cubicle. She took a step. Stopped. Looked inside the cabin again. Mr Achyutan’s back was towards her. “This is it! He won’t see me go out.”

Her next stop. Toilet. She tip-toed through the gallery. A pungent smell invaded her nose like invisible little Roman soldiers, destroying everything in its path and settling inside the fort to takeover her brain. “Eww” she was convinced her head vomited a little. She had spent 2 years 5 months and 21 days out of her 2 years 5 months and 23 days in the company avoiding coming here. Officially it was for all employees, but the blame for its, literal, decay were the males of the species. She wanted to get out of there as soon as possible, but this was the only place in the office not visible from the cabin.

She looked at the main door. It was 15ft from the toilet. She calculated that it would take her 15-20 seconds to walk out; if she moved fast, she could cover the distance in 5 seconds. However, the office was unusually quite, and running would surely bring notice to her. Quickly she made a decision to walk out on her toes without making any noise.

Her heart started racing. She could feel a thousand wild buffaloes running in her chest. Tiny beads of perspiration formed on her forehead. She stopped hearing sounds. Her ears were hot.  
She took her first step then another. “Another 10 feet.” she thought “Almost there.”
“AAACCHHHHUUU”

Sharmita froze.

A couple of months ago somebody sneezed in the office and Mr Achyutan, came running out of his cabin to greet what he thought was his mother coming for a surprise visit.
Anyway, since that day, it became a running joke.
Sneeze. Wait for the lecture. Giggle.

Sharmita realized she had been standing on her toes for longer than what was required from ballet dancers on a good days work.
“Stupid. Stttuuupid. Stuuuupppiidd” she thought “What should I do now? Did he see me? Should I look to check?”
Her thoughts were louder than usual. She had to speak up over the sound of her heart beating.
“Move at least... Any direction... Don’t just stand there.”

This was defeat. She held the papers in both hands. Turned, wiped the perspiration on her forehead, and walked back.

“Mr Achyutan.” she said as he came out of his office, phone in his left hand, his right hand trying to block the obscenities, he was about to shout, reaching the other side.
“I think I took your copies by mistake. I thought there were mine.”

He looked at her, signalled her to follow him to the cabin and started talking on the phone.
“Nothing. Office kids. Mmhmm, mmhhmm. Yeah, no probelm. I’ll be there in half hour.”

“Ms Singhal. What do you want?” he turned towards Sharmita.
“Just these papers. I picked them up from the copy machine thinking they were mine. I was thinking of going through the Digitech presentation I have tomorrow.” said Sharmita.
“Stop explaining so much. It sounds like you’re lying. SHUT UP and hand him the papers” she thought.

“hmmm. Come back to my office later. I’ll review the Digitech presentation. We don’t want any mistakes. Do we?” said Mr Achyutan. He wasn’t expecting an answer from Sharmita. She nodded her head anyway.
“Take these pages with you. I’ve jotted down some points for the Mahishasur Pitch. Research the company. Go to the market to check out competition. Conduct a few interviews with customers.” He paused for a moment to look for his keys. “The pitch is Monday next week. I want this done by Friday so that I can review it.”
Sharmita’s grip on the pages tightened. Her ears were getting hot again. She struggled to stop her thoughts from coming out of her mouth. She knew what was coming.
“We don’t want any mistakes. Do we?” said Mr Achyutan as he walked out.

She looked at the pages again. “I can’t use anything from here”. She figured she will have to do everything. In 3 days.

Sharmita stood there for a few seconds. Turned around, threw the pages in the dustbin and walked out of the cabin. Crossing the toilet through the passage she finally breaching the main door.

........

“How much?” Sharmita asked as she handed the shopkeeper 13 Rs for the cigarette. He took the money and nodded. This had become a routine for her. Everyday, once before lunch and once before evening tea, she would go down the building, walk up the road to the small shop.

She thought about how routine her life had become. Everyday she would get up at 8:30 after snoozing the alarm twice. She had to reach to the office by 9.30. Fumbling through her morning routine of ultra mild, shower, tea and bowel movements, she would always manage to leave by 9, only to get late because the uber driver couldn’t find the location, autorickshaws suddenly realized they deserved double the amount they normally charged, or Bangalore traffic.

Her thoughts were broken by the vibration in her pants. She did not want to talk to anyone right now. She looked at the phone. It was Pradeep. Hesitatingly she picked up.
“Hello”
“Out for a smoke break. Hmmm. No, I’ll eat in an hour.”
She took a couple of drags “ummm....Pradeep...” she paused. An argument is the last thing she wanted. “... is it okay, if I am not able to come tonight” She was hurrying through her cigarette now.
“I have a lot of work” she did not want to deal with him tonight. “I don’t know. I have a couple of pitches to work on.”
“Not tonight. Please. Please. Please.” she screamed inside
“uh-huh... uh-huh... But I might get late. you’ll have to wait a while.” her voice had softened.
“uh-huh...okay, If you want to. There’ll be a spare key with the guard.”
“cool. Bye.”

She disconnected the phone and looked at the screen.

7 unread messages from dad. She was avoiding talking to both of them today. She knew what they wanted from her was not something she was ready for.

She looked at the cigarette. Ash hanging. Tobacco all but smoked.

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/One_Year_After_Triptych.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (prose | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Contrasting how three people survive the one-year anniversary of a death through paranoia, reluctant physical intimacy, and an unexpected pregnancy
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested Pooja's half-asleep name slip to Craft_and_Resistance_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Strip the first-person editorializing from Act I and stage the mother's phone paranoia as a live scene
> - [ ] Expand Act III: Mannu's reaction to Sarita's pregnancy announcement with the ring still in his pocket

Date: 12-11-2018
Topic: A year after your death…

Somebody comes home. It’s the anniversary of the day I passed. My mom welcomes them. Makes tea for them. They ask her how she is doing. She says it still doesn’t believe it happened a year ago. She feels guilty for living when her husband and elder son have both died. I wish she didn’t feel that way. These things happen. As much as we like to believe, we don’t have any control. SShe cries, there are tears rolling down her cheeks. It’s been a year now. She is even more paranoid now. She calls Mannu and Vartika everyday. If they don’t pickup the phone, she gets scared. Starts calling all of their friends. She sometimes has suicidal thoughts. But she doesn’t have the conviction. It’s the guilt that makes her think it.
Pooja has moved on. She remembers me fondly. She doesn’t think she’ll ever stop loving me. She thought we’d get married and have a family. Travel the world. Be crazy in love with each other. All those expectations came crashing down. She started dating recently. Went out with a couple of boys. Both setup by someone else. One of her best friends set her up with a guy from her office. He was a nice guy, good job, well traveled and smart. She went out with him a couple of times. She couldn’t bring herself to go out with him again. It was too hard. It had been six months. Today she was going out with Hareesh. Her mother had set her up with him. She had already gone out with him a few times. SHe liked the guy. He was funny and caring. He was sweet. He liked going out with friends to parties, meeting new people. He was social. Complete opposite of me. She couldn’t help but compare him to me. 
That night she had gone out to a nice restaurant. There was a nervousness in her demeanor tonight. It had been a couple of months that she had been going out with this guy. Her mother was keen on knowing whether she wanted to marry the guy or not. Her nervousness was for a different reason. She knew that there was an expectation of physical intimacy. Her friends had told her that she needed to do it. It had been too long. A year is a long time to have not had sex. She decided to go for it. 
At the end of the night, he had come to her apartment to hang out. It was a normal occurrence. He had come to Pooja’s apartment a bunch of times and gone home disappointed. Tonight seemed a little different to him though. She felt relaxed and open to his touches. She made him a drink and was smiling at him throughout the night. They put on a movie on the TV. He snuck in close to her and put his arm around her — making sure he wasn’t touching her. Just kept it on the sofa gently. Pooja could see what he was upto. SHe didn’t mind. She put her head on his shoulder. He put his hand on her arm. She looked at him. They both wanted it. He kissed her. Gently at first. Then hurridely they moved to her room. They made love. She felt nice. She fell asleep after that. He hugged her from behind. Half asleep, she smiled and said, “I love you Tarun.”
Mannu had been dating the girl for a year and half now. He wants to get married. He had been ready for it for a long time now. But there was a desperation now. He wanted to make mom happy. He thought this was the best way he could do it. She would be really happy to see him get married. He was planning to propose. He wanted to get married and have children as soon as possible. He had picked up a ring last week and was planning something special tonight. He had cooked baked chicken and bread. He had ordered a chocolate mousse for dessert. He had decorated the balcony, where they were having dinner. Somewhere in the middle of dinner he would get down on one knee and propose. 
Sarita, came on time. He hugged her and gave her a kiss. Made her a cocktail with lemon vodka, white rum, crushed blueberries and soda. They talked while having dinner. Actually he seems to be talking. She looks a bit distracted. Her drink was still there. She had barely had a sip. Mannu held her hand and told her that he wanted to talk about something important. She stops him. SHe says there is something she wants to say first. Mannu checks the ring in his pocket by tapping on it from outside his jacket. It’s still there. She says, “I don’t know how you will take this. I don’t want you to hate me after this. I thought I could say it. But it’s so damn difficult”. Mannu gets worried. He doesn’t know what to expect but has an idea. Fearing the worst, he takes a deep breath and asks her to go on. “I’m just gonna go ahead and say it. I’m pregnant.”

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Archaeological_Time_Heist.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Stealing artifacts from ancient history, burying them in local soil, and exhuming them in the present to live comfortably in a small 2-bedroom house playing video games until a timeline paradox strikes
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Map the narrative beats onto Harmon's story circle (Comfort -> Need -> Unfamiliar -> Adapt -> Get -> Pay -> Return -> Change)
> - [ ] Detail the thief's mundane double life: millions in escrow while eating instant noodles in a 2BHK flat
> - [ ] Establish the inciting incident: an altered bronze inscription that erases a key modern memory

Date: 12-03-2019
Topic: Write about stealing something.


I used to spend precious time in front of the mirror. Brushing my hair. Putting oil and cream. Setting it one way then changing it to something else. Changing it back again. I was obsessed about my hair. It was as if I knew I was going to lose them. Maybe my over attachment to them made them leave me. 
I used to think my friends had better hair. They had more of it or had a better style that I couldn’t do on my hair. I used to think of stealing their hair. It was silly. I thought maybe if magic existed. And I had the gift. I could just think about swapping our hair, and it would be done. So I would do exactly that. I would sit close to a friend whose hair I wanted. Look at their head. And utter these words in my head: “I wish I get your hair”
Obviously it didn’t happen. But if it did, imagine the surprise on their faces. They would know something strange is going on. But how would they ever know what. THey could even observe something strange about my new head. But what would they say? “You stole my hair”. How? How exactly did I steal them without you knowing. 
Have I ever wanted to steal something. I watch movies like Ocean’s eleven and the Italian job. They make stealing look so cool and glamorous. Stealing paintings and money. Doing it in style. 
I’ve thought about doing something like that. Not to do it in real life, of course. I was thinking from a story perspective. A heist movie, or a con job. How would I pull it off? I have to start with what I want to steal. I’m not interested in paintings. I don’t know anything about them. Same with antique pieces. I can probably make something about antiques. It can be about history. 
How about a story about a person who has the ability to travel in time. This person — let’s call this person X — goes back in time to different time periods. Steals objects like utensils, statues, ornaments. Hides them near the area. Goes back to their own time. Digs the stuff up and sells them. 
That’s an interesting idea. Why does he do it? For money. X is smart. He has to think about keeping his secret. So, he does it discreetly. He has millions of dollars, but he lives in a smallish 2 bed room house. He doesn’t need to steal anymore. But he keeps doing it.
How would the story go. Where is the conflict? What does he want? X lives alone. Has a few friends who hang out with him. They watch TV and play video games. 
Maybe he goes back and changes something that has an effect on the present time. I’ll have to do this on a story circle. That will give me some idea on how can the story move forward. 
What are the images I see. Mohan jo daaro. Akbar. Babar. Swords. 
So this person is going to change things in the present time. By stealing something. That loses it’s value because of that change. But that is not enough. He loses something important. SO he has to go back and change things. I’ll study the story circle again and try to put it through that process.

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Astrological_Chink_in_the_Armor.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Pundits claiming astrology is math to avoid claiming prophecy; relatives hyping a home pooja as a showdown between an engineer cynic and a famous astrologer, while the narrator stubbornly refuses to travel abroad just to prove the horoscope wrong
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Build out the tension between family faith and the cynical son's interrogation
> - [ ] Ironize the fact that he later travels the world, proving the astrologer accidentally right

Date: 21-03-2019
Topic: Write about a fortune teller.

I’ve often wondered if the people who claim to know the future, believe they really can see the future. Pundits in Hindu religion are one such people. But they don’t claim to know the future. You can’t make every pundit claim that they can see the future. One of them is going to tell the truth. They’ve done a really clever thing instead. They tell people they can tell your future by analyzing the position of the stars based on your birth. This way they don’t have to claim to have the magical power of foresight. And they can say it involves math and calculations. Which somehow justifies it in the mind of people who believe in it. Most people don’t even need that justification.
I’ve had my fortune told by many pundits. Most people have said I’d do really well in life. Which is subjective really. I was studying to be a software engineer. I lived a privileged life and had good prospects. And my mom would consider a steady job with regular promotion, ‘doing well in life’. Many of them have also said that I would travel to foreign countries. Travel is getting cheaper. More and more people travel to other countries. Most of my friends have traveled to Europe, Asia, America. I doubt that their pundits would say the same for them. It’s a generic thing to assume, that most people living a certain lifestyle will travel. However, I like to prove my fortune tellers wrong. I’ve never ever been outside the country. My friends, whose pundits never predicted a foreign trip, have been multiple times.
Last year my mom wanted to get a pooja done. She had invited a famous pandit to our house to conduct pooja and read our fortune. I am a cynic. So, I wanted to test this guy out. But I had to fly to Bangalore in a couple of days. My cousins and relatives urged me to stay back. They wanted to see the exchange between me and the pundit. THey thought that either he will convert me, or I will find a chink in his armor. Even I wanted to talk to him and try to debunk him. Unfortunately I had to go. Maybe I’ll get another chance. Maybe not. I doubt he would have converted me into a believer. I don’t know if I would have debunked him or not. I’ll make a prophecy right now. Someday, I’ll meet this pundit, and I’ll find out how he makes people believe in his prophecies.

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Bandra_Seaface_Dawn_and_the_Hanging_Bridge.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Waking on the rocks at 5 AM unable to lift heavy eyelids; watching a couple make out passionately while joggers pass; staring at the Sea Link glowing red and yellow as if hung from a single nail in the sky
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Refine the heavy eyelids sensory metaphor (Olympic lifter)
> - [ ] Anchor the urban public/private space clash of lovers kissing on Mumbai rocks
> - [ ] Connect to [[Memories_Underfoot_Bombay_Locals_and_Mahabaleshwar_Strawberries]]

Date: 18-04-2019
Topic: You’re asleep. You’re not at home.

I can hear people talking from time to time. I can hear the cars racing away. Even though my eyes are closed. It is almost as if I can see the streetlight over my head. I see a man who look like me do strange things. Fly. Run around a cricket ground. Sing and dance. Have sex. But my eyes are closed. 
I try opening my eyes. But the eyelids are too heavy. How did they become so heavy suddenly. I open and close them thousands of times a day. And right now, it seems like a job for an Olympic lifter. I have awareness of my surroundings. I can hear the sea crash on the rocks. Somebody just jogged past me. Maybe it was two people. I could hear the footsteps. Rhythmic. Someone must be jogging. 
I am definitely asleep. Am I awake as well? In this moment, I am both. I force my body to sit. It is easier that opening my eyes. I sit like this for a while. Eyes closed. Hands resting on the side. Trying to hold my body in place. Focus on your breathing. Breathe in. Breathe out. Breathe in. Breath out. The see air hits the back of my head. Its cold. I’m sweating. I’m fully awake now. I can open my eyes.
There is a couple sitting not far from me. I can see their faces. They are kissing. Not like a peck on the lips. Intense, passionate kissing. One that I thought you’re supposed to do at home. Or in private. Where is their home. Don’t they want any privacy. Are they okay with people looking at them. The guy, who is facing me, sees me staring. They stop. The girl lays down. Keeps her head in the guy’s lap. I look away. 
It’s 5 o’clock. Still two hours to go. More people come jogging. I turn around. I want to look at the sea. The rocks below me are barely visible. I can see a bridge. It looks beautiful. Red, yellow, orange lights illuminating it. It looks like it is hanging by threads. Tied to a single nail somewhere in the sky. The couple start kissing again. There is still some sleep in my eyes. I’m dazed. My gaze gets stuck wherever I look. I can’t help it. The guy sees me again. This time they don’t care.

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Bangalore_FIFA_PlayStation_Heist.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (prose | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Committing a residential burglary purely to escape selfish controller gatekeepers, only to inherit the stolen console and become the exact same selfish gatekeeper
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested burglary justification and full-circle controller dialogue to Voice_and_Dialogue_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Format dialogue blocks cleanly with character beats
> - [ ] Connect to [[EBOLA_Engineer_Bhai_Dating_Guru_Sketch]] and [[The_Appraisal_Riots_Sudhir_Kumar_Sketch]]
> - [ ] Sharpen the dumpster scene where they discover Rob's American Tourister bag

Date: 18-12-2018
Topic: When I awoke the next morning…

When I awoke the next morning, there was no one in the room. I looked around to see everything cleaned up. It was almost like before we had come into it. Almost. Except, I was there. 
I’m at Rob’s place? What the fuck am I still doing at Rob’s place. What happened? Where is Chris?
 
I tried to think about the previous night. We had wanted to come into this house for a couple of months now. Not to steal anything mind you. We’re not thieves. We just wanted to play Fifa on the dude’s playstation. Now I know you might think that it is a stupid reason to break into somebody’s house. But it wasn’t like we planned it. I should clarify first — by we I mean me and my pal Chris. 
Chris had tried to establish a friendship with the guy. We thought maybe we could be invited to play if we’re friends with him. Chris was not very good at these things though. But at least he was better than me. Unfortunately, we needed him to do better than he was doing. He kept bumping into the guy in the cigarette shop whenever we could spot him from our window. 
About a week ago, this guy, lets call him Rob, was walking back to his house. He had a lot of packets in his hands. Chris spotted him from the window and ran down. After initially refusing the help, Rob gave some of his stuff to Chris. Chris had already messaged me to be ready. He had been invited into the house for a smoke.
They talked about work, life and weather in general. Trying to make small talk — which Chris is horrible at. He kept bringing up the playstation — asking what kind of games he had. He hung around awkwardly for a few minutes after they had smoked. Rob told him he was going out of town for a couple of weeks.
Chris came home disappointed. He thought he would at least get to play one game. Anyway, we decided to forget about the situation now and pick it up when the dude returns.
We come back to last night now. We had gone for a party to a friend’s fiance’s place. To our surprise we found he had a playstation as well. We had to spend a few hours at this place and we were sure that we will get to play for a little bit. 
Excited about the prospect of playing Fifa, we drank and socialized. A couple of hours into the party, the fiancee switched on the console, and started playing Fifa with his roommate. I asked if I could play a game or something. The roommate was an asshole. He told us that he doesn’t like to share his controller with anybody. What will anyone do the controller? Kidnap it? Give it AIDS. Asshole. I turned to the fiancee. He was a little more sharing. A little not much. He said, “Sure man. If I get beaten You can take the controller.
I decided to talk to Sonam. She was one of the Fiance’s work colleague. I don’t know what we talked about, I just kept looking at the game going on. She was gorgeous. But I really wanted to play fifa. On any other day, I would’ve been way into this girl — not today.

We played board games, drinking games, pictionary. All the while my attention completely on Fifa. During pictionary, Sonam turned to me and said “You know if you’re really interested in doing something about that thing, you should give me your number. We’ll get in touch” 
“About what?” I looked at her blankly.
She smiled and said, “About the charity stupid. We were talking about it. You were really interested in doing something.”
I nodded my head. Still looking at Fifa. “Yeah. I mean. Sure.”
“Let’s do something. I’ll give you my number and you can call me.” 
“Sure. Sure.” I said.
“Or not. Even if you want to talk or… You know chill sometime.” 
I couldn’t believe my ears. I was so obsessed with Fifa that I didn’t notice that this girl was into me.
“Really?” I said like a person who couldn’t believe his luck. “That would be great.”
“My turn.” A random dude with funny glasses ran towards the console. My head turned towards him. There was this girl, that could be a model one day if she decided. Giving me her number. To talk and to chill. And all I could think was that it was my turn to play.
“Okay, here, take this.” She gave me a piece of paper. There was a number on it. I assume it was hers. SHe smiled at me.
“Thanks, I’ll definitely give you a call. Can you excuse me for a minute” I said as I stood up and went to the console. The new guy was a couple of goals down. I was excited as I would finally get to play.
As expected the new guy got beaten. I tool the controller from his hands, and started selecting my team.
“Dude it’s my turn” the fiance said, coming out of nowhere.
What the fuck is his problem. “But that guy just lost. And you lost before…”
“Dude I get the turn after whoever loses.” He replied.
“But…”
“You can play after me dude. For sure. I’ll give the controller to you. I promise.”

It was not my apartment. It was not my console. I had no choice but to give it back to him. I got up and stood behind the chairs.
The next hour I saw the Fiancee beat the roommate in every game they played. At one point I saw him take a team from the Korean league and still beat his Barcelona. Others at the party had also asked for a turn. But the stipulation was repeated to them. People had started getting frustrated at this point, and started to leave. I looked around to spot Sonam, but even she had left. Seeing no sign of either of these guys giving up, Chris and I decided to leave as well.  
I should tell you that we had been drinking for 3 hours now. Add about 3 joints to the equation, and we were not in our minds. Going home in the cab, both of us were commiserating each other on how fucked people are. Empathy, kindness and sharing were qualities of an extinct breed of men.
I can’t tell you what came over me, because I don’t do shit like I did a few minutes later. I told the driver to stop at Rob’s house. Chris thought we had reached back to our place. Rob’s apartment was on the ground floor. I thought it wouldn’t hurt anybody if I had a peak inside. It was just a harmless peak. Nothing more. 
I looked in the apartment, it was dark. I could see the outline of the television from the faint streetlight coming into the apartment. I cupped my hands and put them on my eyes to see better. The window was loose, it opened with just a little push. 
My heart was racing. I told myself, I shouldn’t do what I was about to do. But there was this other voice that kept telling me how unfair the world is. How people with playstations are assholes. They have their console with them all day everyday, yet they are unwilling to share it with anyone else. The argument did it’s job. We weren’t doing it to a good person. We were doing it to the Assholes of the world. I climbed in. 

Chris was standing right behind me waiting for me to carry him inside. On seeing me climbing up the window, he got confused. He looked around, his face becoming red. He asked me what the hell was I doing. Suddenly he was completely sober. We argued for less than a minute — after which he climbed in. It wasn’t as if my arguments convinced him, it was mainly the fear of leaving me alone breaking into somebody else’s apartment. He wasn’t going to leave his buddy. 
I told Chris that we will play a couple of games and then leave. And this is what I truly believed in the moment I said it. But it was before Chris beat me two games in a row. 
A couple of hours later, and after being satisfied with how the night had gone, we decided to leave. But I wanted water first. There was no cold water in the fridge, but it was filled with Hoegarden. I picked one, and started drinking. Chris looked at me. I picked up another one and brought it to Chris. We decided to play a couple more games.
It was 3AM. We were drunk out of our minds. We had gotten food delivered. Neat trick — we waited outside to receive the delivery. There were boxes all over the floor, food on the furniture, and it looked like Chris had puked in the corner. 
Chris’ eyes were closing. Even I felt incredibly sleepy. The last thing I remember I was trying to talk Chris into leaving before anyone sees us. And now this morning. Chris gone. Everything back in it’s place. Clean.
***************
The door was slightly ajar. I left through the window. I didn’t have my mobile on me. I ran back to my apartment. I wanted to ask Chris what happened. Chris sat at home chilling. He got up on seeing me.
“What happened dude?” He asked.
“What do you mean what happened? I just got up and left? You tell me what the fuck happened.”
“Dude there was a lady at the door. She had a key. I went out the window. There was no time to wake you up.”
“There was nobody inside when I got up. Did she call the police?”

After sharing all the information between us. The story we had was, both of us had passed out after drinking all the beer from the fridge. At some point in the night I had shifted to Rob’s bedroom and put Rob’s blanket on myself and drooled over Rob’s pillow.
In the morning. A bell woke Chris up. He panicked. He could see an older woman on the door. He came to the bedroom to wake me. He snatched the blanket from my body and gave me a couple of shakes. He would’ve have tried even harder but the woman had a key and she was about to open the door. He put the blanket over my body and left through the window. Something happened in an hour between then and when I woke up. And after that, you know the story already.
We had a lot of questions and a few answers of our own.
Who was that woman? Probably the maid. Could be his mother.
If it was either of those why didn’t she call the police? Maybe she saw the blanket over me and thought it was Rob.
Seems unlikely. But can happen. The blanket was probably on my face. I can’t really remember.

Chris wanted to let it go. A part of me did as well. 
“Where is my jacket?” I suddenly remembered. “Fuck. I think I left it at Rob’s place.”
“Forget it dude. It was a cheap jacket anyway.”
“But it had that girl’s number in it. What was her name…”
“Sonam” replied Chris.
“Yeah that’s it. I’ve got to get that number back.”
“Dude. It’s pure luck that we didn’t get arrested. We can’t go back now. What will we even say?”

I somehow convinced Chris to at least observe from a safe distance. We can see if there is any chance to get the jacket back. I changed my clothes and took Chris back to Rob’s apartment. 

We lurked outside. There was no police there. We went towards the window. There was a large parking area where we could observe from a distance. The older woman was sitting inside watching TV. 
“What’s your gameplan here?” asked Chris after 20 minutes. “You can’t stare at somebody’s window all day.”
He was right. I didn’t have a plan. I was hoping somebody would just come and tell us the whole story. 
Hey you know somebody broke into the house last night. Rob’s mother had come in the morning and thought it was Rob sleeping. Cleaned the house to find him gone. He called her and found out Rob is in another city.”
But nobody came. I was hoping for too much. 
“Chris. You know, our theory about this lady being Rob’s mom.” I finally said.
“Yeah.”
“Why wouldn’t his mom know he is out of town?”
“Because…” Chris seemed unsure “… maybe she thought he came home a week early. Maybe they don’t talk much. Could be anything man.”
“Does that sound like any moms you know?”
Chris struggled with it for a while, then said, “Yeah well. Still, we can’t stand here all day.”
Chris was right again. I had to either let it go or do something.
“Come with me.” I said. He obliged. We went to Rob’s door and asked him to knock. Chris didn’t think this was a good idea. But after explaining to him that there is no way she would recognize either of us he agreed. I told him what to say, when she opens the door.
“Hello.” The older lady answered the door. She looked like a mom. But not of a 25 year old guy. She looked close to 40, wearing jeans and T-shirt.
“Hello auntie. I’m Chris.” Chris said “Is Rob in?”
“He is not here right now.” She looked annoyed at something.
“When will he be back?”
“I don’t know.” We stared blankly at each other’s faces. It looked like there was nothing else Chris could ask her.
“Are you his mom?” I said, gathering some courage. 
She looked at us. It looked like she was thinking about the answer.
“No.” She finally said.
That wasn’t the answer I was looking for. If she said she was his mother, I would’ve left knowing that my theory was probably right. This woman didn’t look like she was his mom, but how can you really tell somebody’s age. 
She had had enough of us. She gave us a fake smile and was about to close the door.
“I’m sorry. I’m sure this is getting annoying for you. But who are you?”
She looked in no mood to answer me. She held the door half open and just stared at me annoyed.
“We’re Rob’s friends. We have never seen you around. So…” I was struggling to complete and she was in no hurry to help me out.
“Hasn’t Rob told you.” She said “I thought you were his friends.”
“Yeah. I mean… You know… We’re not that close. We’ve just hung around a couple of times” Chris said.
“Well call him ask.” 
We nodded our heads like idiots. Not knowing what to say or do. “You do have his number, right?” She said.
“No, we’ve just met around the place.” Chris finally said.
“Listen, as far as I’m concerned, you guys are strangers. And I’m not comfortable talking to you and revealing details about myself.” She tried closing the door again.
“Wait, wait” I remembered my jacket, “I left my Jacket here the other day. Can I at least take it back”
“Black, with a zipper?”
“Yes”
“Hooded”
“Yes”
“Yeah, I threw it out.”
“What? You threw out somebody else’s jacket. How can you do that? It’s not yours to throw.”
“Listen, whatever your name is, this is my apartment, and I will do as I please with the stuff inside.”
“Your apartment? I thought this was Rob’s place.”
“Not really. He was living with me…” she pause, took a deep breath and said “Living here for a couple of months.”
“He was? Isn’t he living with you… I mean living here now?”
“Not after the shit he and his friends pulled last night.”
Oh man, she thinks Rob did it. Should I tell her. Fuck no. She threw my jacket. What kind of person does that. I looked at Chris. He had a smirk on his face. I felt a little guilty.
She continued, “Listen, if you want your jacket, it’ll still be in the dumpster outside the building. If the garbage truck has not already taken it.”
Chris breathed a sigh of relief. Thanked her and pulled me out towards the dumpster.

************

“Dude why did you pull me from there?” I asked Chris on our way to the dumpster. 
“You got your jacket. Well sort of. Remains to be seen.” He replied.
“But we don’t even know who that woman was.”
Chris had a huge smile on his face. 
“What?” I asked, “Why are you so happy?”
“Dude, that was his Sugar Mama.” 
“Sugar Mama” 
“Dude,a sugar mama is a woman who…”
“I know what a sugar mama is. Couldn’t that be his landlady.”
Chris laughed at my suggestion, “Were you even there dude? She was his Sugar mama.”
Chris liked the conclusion he had reached. I didn’t care anymore. As we reached the dumpster we saw a brand new American Tourister bag right on top. It seemed strange so we pulled. I laid down a couple of bricks to stand on to look inside the dumpster. Chris stood outside, examining the bag, “Dude, it says ‘Property of Rob Abraham’.” I looked back, got down from the dumpster. It did belong to Rob. 
I don’t think either of us planned to leave it alone. Now, I don’t consider myself a particularly lucky person. And going by the day I was having up till now, I was not hoping for anything interesting. It opened right away. She hadn’t locked it. Why would she.
Chris opened the bag. “What the fuck.” I looked at Chris and mouthed those words. His eyes went so wide that I would’ve been scared if I hadn’t looked inside the bag. “Close it. Close it.” I told Chris, “let’s go home.”
****************
“Dude. This is so cool.” this was one of my work people — Sanjay, “When did you get it.”
“Today,” said Chris looking at me. I giggled. 
“Where did you get it from,” asked Sanjay again.
Chris and I looked at each other. Blank face. We’d been playing for the past hour and a half now. Our eyes had closed from staring at the screen for so long. But for that one moment both of us smiled and said “Assholes”
I decided to throw a party to end the day. Nothing fancy. Just a few of my friends from college. An ex girlfriend. A couple of work people. We had alcohol. Music. We had finger food delivered from a budget restaurant nearby. And the best of all — we had Fifa.
“Hey can I get a turn?” asked Sanjay.
“Uhmmmm, sorry man… I ummm… don’t share my controller with anyone.” Chris replied.
“Ummm… yeah sure man. If I get beaten, you can take the controller from me,” I told Sanjay as he looked at me expectantly, “Deal?”
“Sounds good.” Sanjay stood up and went to the kitchen to fix himself a drink.

“Lose to me in the last 10 minutes?” I whispered to Chris.
“Deal.”
******************************

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Bangalore_Rental_Lucky_Room_Streak.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (prose | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Moving into a bedroom mythologized by the maid for guaranteeing marriages and job offers, only to leave broke and unemployed
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested lucky room streak break quote to Voice_and_Dialogue_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Connect to [[The_Urbanclap_Bed_and_Lost_Autonomy]] and [[Daily_Characters_Observation_List]]
> - [ ] Develop the Bangkok traders' abandoned apartment mystery

Date: 31-12-2018
Topic: You're moving into a new house; write about the people or person who lived there before you.

I love this house. Especially the big rooms. I had been looking for an apartment for 3 months now. This house was worth the wait. The balconies are spacious. The bathrooms big enough. 
There were three girls living in this house before us. I got the house through them. I had been looking for a long time. While I was looking, one of my friends moved into this house. But she decided to move out in a month, because these girls weren’t ever here. 
These girls lived in bangkok now. They had been paying for this house for a while. Which doesn’t make sense. They must’ve been doing well it seems. TO be making the kind of money to pay rent in two places. And the rent here wasn’t less. It was 14K a month. Which leads me to believe that they were either being lazy to make the decision to move out, or they had enough money to believe they could continue paying for it and have an option of returning here. 
I don’t really remember there names. I was told their stories by Bhagya, the house maid. She had been working there for a few years. The girls were friends. Two of them or maybe all three were partners in an online business. Selling clothes and accessories in Thailand. They were doing well. One of their partners lived next door. He was always out. Working. I don’t want to presume that the girls gave him a shitty deal. I don’t know enough. But in this scenario, I would want to be the one living in Bangkok.
The house was kept in decent condition. But that seems to happen when you’re not living there, and a maid comes everyday to clean. There were a lot of things in the house that they had left behind. We sent most of the stuff next door. There was a dresser, beds, mattresses. I think there was a bean bag as well. 
I never met them. I had talked to one of them a few times on the phone. Discussing the moving in/out situation, and dealing with the landlord. 
The house had been rented out for a number of years now. Bhagya was the only constant. She had told me about the people living there. I don’t remember most of it. I should pay more attention to when people are telling me stuff. It’s interesting when I think it is. Bhagya told me that my room was lucky. The girl who lived there before me wanted to get married. And she did a few months after moving into the room. A boy who was living there got a better job. Similar things happened to other people in the room. I’m not too proud of having broken the streak. When I got out, I was unemployed, broke, and no idea what to do. 
Akansha, my friend, lived for a month there. I had been looking for houses with her. Then she found this one and ditched me. But I can’t fault her for that. It had been a while. Also, she redeemed herself by telling us about this house when she was moving out. She also helped me get the best room in the house. I’m grateful for that. I would have been a stupid little puppy about it.

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Broken_Navy_Cut_and_the_Terminal_Pitch.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Polished Short Story Draft
> **Core Conflict:** Vivek breaks a Navy Cut cigarette, claims cancer to dodge a termination meeting, and begs his oncologist to conceal that it is terminal.

---

From a line:

Most things will never happen; This one will. Vivek thought to himself. He had been trying to quit for a long time now. He has been trying for over four years now. He stopped at the signal,waiting for the cars to pass. His hands tingled as he wanted another one. He checked his pockets even though he knew he hadn’t bought a pack yesterday.
There is a shop at the next corner. Maybe I’ll get one, he thought. No. Not this shit again. How many times will I do this to myself. One becomes two which becomes a pack. No I won’t. 
The signal had the red light on. He crossed the road. Walking determined. Thought about the meeting he had later that day with his manager. “I want to quit. No that’s a little rude. I have to quit. I need to take a break. No None of these work. How can I tell him that I…” He noticed the cigarette shop from the corner of his eye as he was thinking about the meeting. 
Maybe I’ll buy one for later, he thought. I’ll need it if the meeting doesn’t go too well. But you decided… It’s play. Just one more. I can’t quit cold turkey, I need to phase it out.
“Bhaiya ek Navy cut aur ek chutki dena.” He asked the shopkeeper, who duly obliged. He paid him. Emptied the packet of chutki in his mouth and was about to light the cigarette. You were buying this for later weren’t you? If the meeting didn’t go well. He stopped for a moment. You will never be able to follow through with anything in life… Loser. He put the lighter back on the counter, broke the cigarette in two and threw I on the ground. 
Smiling he looked at the shopkeeper, who had a surprised look on his face. “Nahi wo, mai chodne ki soch raha hun.” He explained. The shopkeeper didn’t reply. He continued washing paan leaves as Vivek walked away. 
The shopkeeper sorted the bigger paan leaves from the smaller ones and arranged them on his plate. Vivek hurried back and picked up the broken cigarette. The shopkeeper looked at him. “Wo, mai dustbin me fek dunga.” HE seemed apologetic, “Chutki ke packet ke saath.” flashing the empty chutki packet and rushed off before he could hear the shopkeeper tell him there was a dustbin right there. He did see Vivek throwing something in the dustbin on the street at a distance.

***** 
Vivek rubbed his thumbs in his hands. He sweat from the armpits, even though the ac was set to 20 degrees. He scrolled down the excel sheet mindlessly, not reading anything from it. “Vivek… Vivek…” shouted the man sitting at the top of the table in the conference room. “Ya… yes sir?” Vivek replied hurriedly. “What about the MTI Bank pitch. Are we ready for tomorrow?” The man asked. “Let me check.” Vivek replied, now sitting straight going through his mail. “Kahan gayi?” He mumbled to himself.
“The pitch is tomorrow Vivek, You should be on top of these things.” THe man roared. 
“Yes… Yes sir. I am… Just…. Give me a minute” Vivek replied. THere were 6 more people in the room all looking at Vivek. He tried not to look at them. His ears turned hot. The patch of sweat around his armpit grew.
The man let out a sigh. “Don’t worry about it now, just send the pitch deck to me latest by 1”
“Sure sir.” He said, “It’s done. I’ll send it to you before that...” He said nervously, “…I think.”
The man relaxed in his chair and told everyone to get back to work. 
Everyone collected their stuff and were moving out. This is my chance to talk to him, he thought. He closed his laptop and waited for everyone to clear the room. He saw his manager collecting his stuff as well. I need to say something right now. “Vivek, stay back. I need to talk to you about something.” The manager said. 
Oh no! What now? 
The room cleared. The manger asked him to sit on the chair closest to him. Vivek nervously walked towards his boss. Million things running through his mind. What does he want to talk about? How will I tell him. Should I tell him before he says anything. No. I should wait till he completes.
“Vivek. Have their been issues at home?” the manager asked.
“No. No sir.” He replied, surprised at the question. “Why do you ask?”
“I don’t like to ask these question. Your personal life is your own. But… considering…” manger paused for a brief second. “Are you sure, everything is fine. Health wise?”
“Yeah. Everything is good.” Vivek clutched his thigh.
“The reason I’m asking all this is because, Romila noticed… She told me to… Listen… YOu seem distracted lately. Your work has suffered and…”
“Sir the pitch is ready. I just couldn’t find the…”
“Its not just the pitch Vivek. You rarely say anything in the meetings. Your replies to important mails have been… unsatisfactory. And…” manager stopped before completing his sentence, He thought carefully about the next thing he was going to say, “Listen Vivek. I want to give you the benefit of the doubt, but I have to answer to Romila. If one person in my team is not working…”
“Are you firing me?” Vivek asked, surprised. 
“Listen Vivek. I’ll help you look for another Job. I’ve made a lot of contacts in the industry, and don’t worry about references. We’ll do or best to…”
“But sir, I got two of the biggest accounts this agency…”
“That was last year Vivek. You haven’t brought in any new business since then.”
“What about StyleNinja?”
“Okay, so you got one account, but that was 15 day campaign. It doesn’t even pay the a months salary for even one designer.”
“I’ll do better Satish. Just give me a chance.” 
“I can’t do anything Vivek. I’ve negotiated a good severance…”
“I have cancer.” Vivek interrupted him. Sounding confident for the first time. 
“What?” 
“I have cancer. Lung cancer. I haven’t told anybody yet.”
Satish stared at Vivek for a long time. He realized it had been a long time since he said anything. “I.. I… I don’t know what to say Vivek. How are you doing?”
“I’ve been going for treatments.”
“Sure… Sure… I’m sorry Vivek… I… I feel a little embarrassed. Forget about what I said before. Let me talk to Romila once. Let me see what I can do.”
“Thanks sir.” Vivek replied. “I don’t like to talk about it… So I didn’t tell anyone…”
“I completely understand Vivek. Don’t worry about it.”
Complete silence for a minute. Neither says anything. “Is there anything else Satish?” Vivek finally broke the silence. “I need to finish the MTI pitch deck.”
Satish looks at Vivek angrily but his expressions turn to sympathy, “Sure sure. Just send it to me by the end of the day.”
Vivek grabs his laptop. “And Vivek. Mail me your medical reports as well. We will need it for our records.”
Vivek hesitates for a second. “Sure sir”

************

There are a few people in groups standing next to a tapri. Vivek walks towards it. “Bhaiya ek chai dena.”
“Aj cigarette nahi loge bhaiya.” The shopkeeper asked.
“Nahi bas chai dedo.”
He took the chai and kept it on the platform next to the tapri. He takes out the broken cigarette from his pocket. Holds the two pieces together and lights it, holding the cigarette at the crack. 
“Aur Vivek, abhi bhi yahin? Tu to quit kar raha tha na?” A tall man approached Vivek. 
“Kar diya tha yaar. Lekin kaam ka stress hi itna hai ki chodi nahi jaati.” He takes a drag.
“Mai job ki baat kar raha tha. Tu last time bol aha tha na ki tu quit karega.”
“Haan yaar. Soch to raha hun bahut dino se. Lekin kaam hi itna hai. Karega kaun?”
The tall man looks at him and realizes the answer a couple of seconds later. “Tu.”
Vivek tries to light the bottom part of the cigarette from the top part which is almost over by now. “Haan yaar. Chaar acounts dekh raha hun abhi mai. If I quit now, company bahut mushkil me aa jayegi yaar.”
“Acha… Sher hai bhai tu. Acha chal ek sutta pila.” Vivek takes him to the tapri to buy a cigarette for him.

************

Vivek is standing in the balcony with his mobile in his hands. He is looking at the the name Dr Bisht. He dials it.
“Hello” says Vivek.
“Hello Vivek. Thank god you returned my call. I’ve been trying to get in touch for…”
“Doctor I need a favor from you.”
“Sure Vivek. But you need to come for your treatment.”
“I will doctor. As soon as I get free from work”
“You should not be working Vivek. Any kind of stress is bad for you.”
“I know doctor. I have quit my job. But I will have to serve a month’s notice period.”
“Chalo. That’s good. But how can your company make you serve a month’s notice. Don’t they know your condition.”
“That’s why I called you doctor. I need my medical reports. I need to submit them to my company.”
“Ah. Sure sure Vivek. When will you come to collect…”
“I’m not free doctor. Lot’s of work. I told you.” Vivek bites on his lips. “Can you mail them to me.”
“Vivek I was hoping you will come in for a checkup at least.”
“I will doctor. But first can you send me my reports?”
“Haan sure. Text me your email address.”
“Will do… And doctor…” Vivek hesitates for a second. “Can you… not mention that…” Vivek bites on his lip again. “ Can you not mention that it is terminal.” There is silence on the other side of the phone, “Doctor?”
“We can still do something Vivek. You don’t need to give up…”
“Can you do it doctor?”
“We can start the chemotherapy as soon as you come in. And who knows…”
“Doctor. I don’t have much time.” Vivek contorts his face for poor choice of words. A smile comes across his face, “I mean I need to get back to…”
“I got it Vivek. I’ll send your reports.”
“Thanks doc…” Vivek slaps his forehead. Trying to be cool was never his strong suite, “..tor”

**************

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine
> **Core Spark:** A tearful plea for family protection flipping into cold mutual blackmail at a highway dhaba
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested blackmail dialogue exchange to Dialogue_and_Voice_Scraps.md
> - [x] Automatically fenced into native Fountain screenplay format
> - [ ] Map the history of the Brijesh Sharma incident: what did Sumedha cover up?
> - [ ] Develop the tension in the second half of the road trip to Dehradun

```fountain
The story is about two cousins. Sumedha is 32 and married. Rishabh is 31 and married with one child. Their mothers were sisters. They grew up in the same city. Went to the same school. Sumedha was in a grade above Rishabh. Since they didn’t have any siblings of their own, they thought of each other as their own. As they grew up, their lives have taken them in different directions. Both have become successful in their careers. Rishabh, became Vice President of an advertising agency. He lives in Mumbai. Sumedha became a IPS officer. And has been working in the police force for the past 4 years. She currently lives in Delhi.
They still talk on the phone from time to time. For the past five years they’ve seen each other only at family functions. Now they are meeting again for another wedding in the family. They had to go a couple of days before the actual wedding to help out with the arrangements and stuff. Rishabh suggested that they drive down to Dehradun. He told Sumedha that they can use this road trip to reconnect.

Sumedha: You want to switch over after the next stop.
Rishabh: Sure.
Sumedha: What happened, I thought you wanted to drive?
Rishabh: Yeah,I’ll drive. 
Sumedha looks at Rishabh as if she is trying to figure out something.
Rishabh: What? I said I’ll drive. Let’s stop at Cheetal. We’ll eat something. Then we’ll switch. Okay?
Sumedha: Do you even want to drive Reezu?
Rishabh: Yeah I want to drive. What is the matter with you?
Sumedha: Dhang se baat kar Reezu. Badi hun mai tujhse. 
Rishabh: Yea kya laga rakha hai aapne didi. Drive karega.Drive karega.
Sumedha: Listen I was perfectly happy with flying. You told me we will do a road trip. You told me we’ll reconnect. You told me this will be fun. 
Rishabh: mmhhhmmm
Sumedha: You have barely said anything for the past two hours. 
Rishabh realizes his mistake. Sits up straight.
Rishabh: Yeah. You are right. I’m sorry. I’ll be better I promise.
Sumedha: Good.Now tell me what do you want to eat.
Rishabh: There is one really nice dhaba nearby. We’ll stop there.

Rishabh: I did want to talk to you about something.
Sumedha: I knew it. Mujhe lag raha tha kuch to chupa raha hai tu. Bata kya baat hai.
Rishabh: Didi. I…
Sumedha: Bol bol.
RIshabh: Didi, I’m under investigation.
Sumedha looks at him.
Rishabh: For sexual harrasment.
Sumedha slows down and stops the car. 
Rishabh: What happened.
Sumedha points at the sign of a dhaba.

They are now sitting on benches outside the dhaba. Sitting on the opposite sides of the table.
Sumedha: Should I ask if you did it?
Rishabh: Of course not Sumi di. I’m innocent.
Sumedha: So why haven’t you told Saloni yet?
Rishabh looks at her surprised. 
Rishabh: I… I didn’t know how to tell her.
Rishabh: I’m going to lose my job Di. Everything I worked for.
Sumedha: Why haven’t you told Saloni?
Rishabh: I had an affair with her.
Sumedha: You what?
RIshabh looks down at his plate. He hasn’t touched the food.
Sumedha: What do you want from me?
Rishabh: Nothing didi. I just… I just wanted to talk to you.
Sumedha: Don’t lie to me Reezu. You want something from me. That’s why you arranged the whole road trip.
Rishabh looks at Sumedha. 
Rishabh: Didi. She has filed a criminal case against me. I’m being charged for sexual assault and rape.
Sumedha: And you want me to?
Rishabh: Do you remember Imran?
Sumedha: Imran from the acedemy?
Rishabh: Yeah. From your batch.
Sumedha: Yeah. I’ve lost touch with him. After the academy. We were good friends.
Rishabh: He is handling my case.
Sumedha: And you want me to…
Rishabh: talk to him…
Sumedha: You know what you’re asking me to do?
Rishabh: Didi, please. My whole life is on the line. I can’t let this go to court.
He starts crying. 
Sumedha puts some money on the table. Gets up and pulls Rishabh to the car.
Sumedha: I’ll drive.

In the car.
Sumedha: TEll me everything that happened. Don’t lie and don’t hide anything.
Rishabh: So will you help me?
Sumedha: Tell me what happened first.
RIshabh: SHe is a senior copywriter in the company. There were some problems between Saloni and I. It started at a client party. I… I slept with her. And it continued for a while. Then after a while things got better at home. Maybe it was the guilt. But Saloni and I actually taking care of each other. Started loving each other. So I stopped. Last month. She filed a complaint with the HR department. She accused me of sexual harrasment. Coercing her into having sex in exchange for promotion.
Sumedha: how long ago was this?
Rishabh: Last year.
Sumedha: Can she prove it?
Rishabh: She has texts.
Sumedha: With you asnking for sex in exchange for a promotion.
Rishabh: No. No.
Sumedha: THen what?
RIshabh: TExts about where can we go. When do we meet. 
Sumedha: Oh Reezu. What is wrong with you.
Rishabh: I’m sorry Di. And if I could take it all back. I would. But I can’t now. What do I do?
Sumedha: Listen Imran is a good police officer. You shouldn’t worry if you hadn’t done anything. Right?
Rishabh: But it will ruin my reputation di. And my family.
Sumedha: I can’t help you Reezu.

Rishabh wipes his tears. Sits up straight. There is silence in the car for a minute.

Rishabh: I know about Brijesh Sharma.
Sumedha: What?
Rishabh: I know how he got out of the murder charge.
Sumedha: He got out because there was no evidence against him.
Rishabh: There was a video.
Sumedha: It was doctored.
Rishabh: There were witnesses.
Sumedha: They died.
Rishabh: All 5 of them?
Sumedha’s face becomes serious.
Sumedha: Are you blackmailing me.
Rishabh: All I’m saying is that you do this for me and I’ll keep my mouth shut.
Sumedha slows down and stops the car again. 
Rishabh: What happened.
Sumedha: You drive.

Rishabh gets out of the car and sits in the driver’s seat.
```

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Cobbler_on_the_Mountain_and_the_Life_Exchange.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** A man asking a mountain cobbler to trade his life; the cobbler points out worn shoes, questions the reality of roses, and reveals the narrator cannot leave his imagination at will, before promising the exchange is possible
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Build out the sensory atmosphere of the barren mountain ridge at eternal sunset
> - [ ] Sharpen the dialogue beats between desperate protagonist and unflappable craftsman
> - [ ] Resolve the ending: what does 'exchanging a life' actually cost?

Date: 02-04-2019
Topic: “I would like to make an exchange”

“What do you want?”, he was repairing a shoe. Piercing the needle through the sole, he hung a string at the top of the needle on a hook, and pulled it. 
“I would like to make an exchange.” I said. This was the first time I had spoken to him. He continued working on the shoe. I cleared my throat and repeated myself, “I would like to make an exchange.”
“I heard you,” he said without looking up, “I didn’t think you were finished.”
He was calm. Working on the shoe. He was good at that. The stitches around the shoe were neat. “I want to exchange my life. I don’t want to live this one anymore.”
For the first time since I met him, he stopped. He looked up at me for a few seconds. He looked at me from up to down and his gaze settled on my feet. “You need to fix those shoes,” he said, “I can fix them up for you if you want.” He looked up at me. His eyes were black with tiny spots of blue and white in them. I couldn’t help but stare at them. 
“It’s fine. I’ll buy new ones.” I jerked myself from the trance. He went back to stitching the shoe in his hand. “I want to exchange my life. I was told you could help me with that,” I said.
“Who told you?” 
“I don’t remember.”
“You don’t remember?”
“I don’t know.” I honestly had no recollection of the person who sent me here. I remember a voice. It was a man. He told me to meet ‘the cobbler’.
“How did you come here?” He dipped a brush in the box of black wax and started polishing the shoe. 
“I came by…” How did I come here? The voice had told me to meet ‘the cobbler’, it had not given me any directions. “I don’t know how I came here.” I looked around. We were on top of a mountain. The sun was setting. It had been setting since I got here. I don’t think it moved. The whole mountain was barren. There was some grass, that a cow was nibbling on. ‘The cobbler’ was sitting with his back on the edge of the hill. 
“What is this place?” I asked. He brought the shoe close to his face and pulled up his nose from the tip. 
“This is really good isn’t it?” He hung his hand out with the shoe in it for me to see.
“Yes. It’s great. Now about…”
“Come closer,” He said. He had a smile on his face. I was standing a meter from him. I took a step forward and bent down. It really was a good job. The shoe was shining. I could see my face on it. I looked at him. He was still smiling. A smile appeared on my lips. He nodded and kept the shoe on the ground.
“Where do you think you are?” He asked.
“Am I dreaming?” I asked back.
“Do your dreams look like this?” He asked again.
I was irritated. He was answering my questions with questions of his own. “I don’t know. I don’t care. Can’t you just tell me where I am?”
“I can. But you wouldn’t believe me.” He said.
“Try me.”
“You are in your head.” He said.
What does he mean? Is he saying this is all in my imagination? Then why does it feel so real? “Why does it feel so real?” I asked him.
“Have you ever seen a rose?” He asked another question. I was getting fed up with his questions. I didn’t want to be rude to him. I had come to him for help.
“No. I have never seen a rose in my life.” I said. He gave out a chuckle.
“How do you know that the roses you’ve ‘never’ seen are real?” 
“I know because… I can touch it, I can see its colors, its form its shape. I can smell its fragrance.”
“But how do you know what you touch, smell and see is the rose.”
“Well, I see it with my eyes, which sends the signal back to my brain and my brain figures out what it is.”
“So your brain tells you it’s real?”
“Yes.” I looked at him anticipating another question, but he just stared at me. 
He was right. My brain makes everything real. So I was imagining this. But I wanted to exchange my life. If this is not real, then that is not going to happen. I should just leave this place. If I am in my imagination, then I can leave anytime I want. Can’t I?
“No,” the cobbler said. 
“What?” I asked.
“You can’t leave anytime you want.”
How did he know that? Can he read my mind? 
“I am in your mind. I am a part of it. If you think it I know it.”
“So I can’t leave. I can’t exchange my life…”
“You can exchange your life.” The cobbler spoke again, “and you can leave here as well.”
I can exchange my life? What is he saying. “How do I…”

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Gerbil_Wheel_and_the_Motorcycle_Jesus.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Feeling like a gerbil on a wheel; investigating extortionist exorcists who mistake rot for flatulence; shooting the head priest between the eyes and blowing up the temple before kicking a motorcycle to life
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Polish the comedic timing of the flatulent exorcist confrontation
> - [ ] Establish the hardboiled tone: leather jacket, desert highway motorcycle getaway
> - [ ] Connect to [[Voice_and_Dialogue_Scraps]]

Sometimes I feel like a gerbil running round and round on his wheel. Doing the same things over and over again. Last night another girl died. If this is what they insist on doing. I don't want to be a party to it. He turned and walked away from Jesus. Jesus ept looking at him. He could see the sun bounce of his shiny head. 
Pf course he is right. Jesus thought. THese exorcists have taken control of the entire neighborhood. They've somehow conviced everybody that there are demons in the area. Are they telling the truth. WHat are demons. I don't believe in demons. But how else can you explaing the possesions.

WHere is the girl's body.  Jesus said to the head exorcist who was sitting with his legs folded and hands on his knees. "WHat body?" THere was a foul smell in the air. Rotting flesh? JEsus thought. No this is not flesh. THe head exorcist lifts his right leg, contorted his face and after being in this position for about 5 seconds, came back to his original position with an "aaah". Jesus was thinking about bodies and that is where his mind went. HE hadn't thought about flatulence. It was obvious now. 
SO where is the body?
I don't know.
THe family doesn't know either. THey say you have it.
You believe everything everyone tells you mister jesus.
Jesus looked at him for a while. SMiled and pointed a gun at him. 
The head exorcist, laughed.
"DO you even know who I am"
BANG!
the shot went right through his forhead. He was fed up of this nonsense. He took a final look at the room. HE could hear his disciples running. He had plans for this situation.
He had locked all the doors to the hall.

Jesus stood outside at a distance. Looking at the temple burning. ROcks and bricks scattered. A few burnt bodies had made it to the door, which blasted open after the bomb went off.
Jesus sat on his motorcycle. Took out the ciggarette box from his pocket and throws it on the ground. "No More"
