DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Inherited_Transistor_Radio.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (prose | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** A chipped radio acting as the final physical relic connecting three generations through morning tea, prayers, and commercial survival
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Connect to [[The_Rispana_Bridge_Floods_and_Black_Cat_Curfew]]
> - [ ] Flesh out the mother managing household finances over the vegetable tempo

Date: 16-01-2019
Topic: Someone’s playing the radio

It has the crackling of static. There is some hymn playing on the radio. It sounds like a prayer. Must be some muslim family getting ready for evening prayers. It’s loud. It seems like it’s far away. I can hear it but the sound diminishes as I walk out of the room. 
Its an old radio. They’ve taken good care of it. It’s chipped at a corner. But polished and cleaned. This would have been in their family for a long time. Or might have been passed down from some friend. I’m making assumptions of course. It is entirely possible they bought it second hand from some shop. 
I would imagine them to have phones though. But not everyone will have access to it. Imagine a household without a TV, that is struggling to keep ends meet. Such a family would not be able to afford mobiles for everyone in the family. They cant afford to waste money on TV and cable bills. They would need a cheaper mode of entertainment. I can imagine them buying a radio for a few hundred bucks.
So if they are playing a channel that broadcasts hymns and prayers, then they would do it pretty regularly. They would do it about 5 times a day. Rest of the time they can play any other entertainment or music channel. There probably would be fights about what gets played. The father probably doesn’t care, except during namaz. THe mother holds the power, and the kids get to negotiate. 
Children don’t listen to the radio that much anyway. They go to their friend’s place to watch TV. Even the mom probably goes to the neighbors place to watch her TV shows. They only listen to it at night. When they are together at home. The father is an orthodox, strict parent. But he is a good man. He works his cart of vegetables everyday, brings the money home, saves for his kids’ education and future. The mom works as a maid. She is a smart, sharp woman. She is extremely observant and action oriented. She keeps the household together. She manages the finances. She earns more than her husband, so she always has money to spend on things for her family. They got married quite young. Mom’s father was a tailor. Dad’s father was a farmer. Their dad’s knew each other from their native village. So, the granddads got together and fixed the marriage. They were both 20.
Granddad (Mom’s father) had this radio that he used to listen to every morning and evening with his tea. He raised mom alone. His wife had been killed in riots. He had a lot of anger in his heart. He wanted to kill the people responsible for his wife’s death. He was okay with dying for the cause. In fact, he was hoping for it. He had a choice between his daughter and revenge. He chose his daughter. He never forgave the people responsible for it. He doesn’t really know their identity. SO his rage is directed at all Hindus. The radio was the last present his wife had given him. He used to drink his morning tea with his wife when she was alive. Now he does it with the radio.
The radio was finally passed down to Mom after her father died. He didn’t have much to pass down to her. He had already given her mom’s jewelery to her for her wedding. Some of which she had sold to manage finances in her house. He had a radio and his shop. Mom and dad had sold the shop to buy the house they were living in. They spent some of the money to buy dad a tempo to sell the vegetables from. He could drive around instead of pushing the cart in the heat.

> **Actionable Next Steps:**

**two incompetent, broke coworkers trying to run a fake kidnapping ring using an intercepted letter while the "victim" is simply enjoying her runaway freedom somewhere off-grid.**

Remember some writing projects that were great at the start but stalled or remain unfinished. Write down why they weren�t completed. Did you talk away any of them? Remind yourself when yo�re discussing a story in progress or an idea for a piece you�ve yet to begin that it�s best to keep quite. Tell yourself you�ll talk about it after the next scene is written, then try to waitfor the scene after that one.

There have been a few ideas that I thought that I would develop into some kind of a story. I talked about it with my friends and nothing happened with them after that. 
I thought about a story of a girl who is unable to meet the demands of life. She is incredibly dissatisfied with her work and her relationship. She runs away. There are these other two people in the story. I thought they would be in her office. THey are in desperate need of money. Accidentally they find out about her running away. They intercept her letter to her father. And build an elaborate ruse, where they pretend to have kidnapped her for money.
I thought this was an interesting premise. But I couldn�t write it. I�ve written a few outlines for this. I even started with the first chapter. Nut I couldn�t continue. I talked about it with a few people. I told the entire premise to Pooja and Amit I think. I told it to Roy as well. 
Over time the main theme of the story has a changed a bit in my mind. I sometimes think the story is about purpose. How to find our purpose. Not how to, but what is purpose basically. How we�re living a purposeless life. I should at least attempt this story. It might be crappy but I think there might be something in it worth exploring.
There are three characters in the story. Three main character. Who approach the central question in three separate ways. One is running away from our problems. Another is trying to find a short cut. Third is just going with the flow.
Someone has to represent inaction as well no? 
There are two main ways people handle this. Do something. And not do anything. Not doing anything leads to dissatisfaction. Whether you are making money, or you�re successful in society�s eyes. YOu�re generally not happy. 
Doing something is still better I feel. It can have two consequences. You fail. You succeed and find a purpose. YOu succeed and find out that even this is not meant for you. SO three mainly. We can�t tackle all of them I think.
So all characters have to take action. Otherwise story won�t move forward. Our protagonist runs away. Do we call that action. Depends on what we call action. I mean. She is running away from her problems. So maybe the action can be defined as confronting your problems. SO she is not confronting her problems. This story is changing themes again I think. 
So this happens quite a few times. I don�t know what I�m writing about. Maybe I should write it out to fing the true meaning of the story. Maybe if she runs away, I�ll know if she was meant to do it.

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Kargil_Colonel_and_the_Brinjal_Plot.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** A father wanting to drown his agony in battlefield death; realizing in the ICU that losing his wife would be worse than losing his son; settling into growing brinjals and smoking weed for nerve pain while an accidental 20kg transit bust looms
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Detail the contrast between the high-altitude battlefield evacuation and quiet Dehradun brinjal farming
> - [ ] Connect to [[Character_Dossiers]] under veteran profiles
> - [ ] Structure the comedic/dramatic meeting between the Colonel and the stranded intern

What do I care about? Something that I care enough about to get my ass off the bed and do something. It's getting ridiculous the amount of time I spend on the bed. WHat do I want to help people with. What irks me the most? I don't know. Nothing irks me man. I don;t care deeply about anything. DO I want to find god? That was an abrupt segue. DO I want to find god? DO I want to solve the god puzzle. How will I go about finding what god is? I'll have to read all religious literature. Understand what different religions consider god to be. DO I already know that. I have a fair idea. DO we call super humans god. WOuld elephant be god then? Would lion?
It's a broad topic. I think I'll have to think about it and talk about it over a period of time. My views may change in the course of time. DO I want to understand the purpoes of life? Is that my book about? IT's about finding oneself. Isn't it? It's about coming into your own. It's about becoming your own person. Taking responsibility for your actions. Isn't she doing that already. BUt She doesn't know what she wants. Because she doesn't know who she is. ANd she is afraid of taking action. She doesn't know her next step. SHe doesn't know what she'll do? Would she just run in this case? Does that seem plausible? THere is a lot of pressure on her. What will she do after running. LEt's say she is doing some travel internship. SHe doesn't reach there on time. Because she missed the train. SO She reaches late. THen she tries to find out the schedule. She decides to go there next and catch up with them. WHat is the best way to reach there. Take a bus. BUt I would want to spend the night here. I don't want to travel at night. SHe gets up in the morning. Buys a ticket. And waits for the bus. THe bus arrives. SHe will soon join the rest of the group and start her internship. She takes the bus and is engrossed in thoughts when there is a stop for a police check post. THe police checks the bus and finds 20 kilos of weed being smuggled. 
They take everybody in custody. Now She is stuck because this will take another day to sort out. But it's okay because the group will be there for a month. We need to put more obstacles. How will she go work with the weed dealer. The weed dealer. WHo is the weed dealer. 
THere is this woman. She is about 60 years old and her husband who is 65. THey used to have a son. Who was in the army. In fact THe man and his son were in the army at the same time. THe son had just joined the army and the man was a 25 year old veteran. THe son had died in the kargil war, they were in the war together. In separate battalions. When the son died, the man was sent back home. They were devastated by the news. THe mother felt a strong pang of pain in her heart. She couldn't breathe. The man was a soldier. He had toughned his heart and body over the years. But even he couldn't find a place to dig and lie down forever. He wanted to be in front of the bullets himself. He just wanted to die. He was a patriot all his life. Services in the armed forces was in his blood. But it was sorrow and a deep need to drown it in his own blood. He did not want to feel this deep pain. THe loss felt worse than death. He wrote a letter and called his superiors to put him back on the battlefield. His wife had told him not to go. But after fighting a few nights. He decided that his wife does not have words to dull down his pain. He left for the war. A few days later she gets a phone call. They tell him that he has been shot and is in the ICU. SHe reaches new delhi to the hospital. THey tell her that he being a colonel didn't have to go directly on the battlefield. But seeing young soldiers being wounded he ran and saved 30 wounded soldiers while under hevay fire, by dragging them one by one to safety.
HIs wife sees him.SHe is relived that he is alive. THere is this sense of pride. He tells her that he loves her and how stupid it was for him to go back. He tells hims that he thought that the pain he felt could only be taken away by death. BUt it's not the same. He tells her that when he was shot he kept going because he wanted to do it for his son. He could see his son in all of them. ANd he felt the pain several times more thinking about the parents who would hear the news. He knew how it felt and for this one moment he was connected with everyone of them. and in this moment all he wanted to do was save his son. So he kept dragging people back. One by one. Even when he was shot. He felt he could move his legs and somehow bring them back.
But when he finally lost consciousness and in this moment he thought he was about to die, all he could see was her. He finally had clarity, worse than losing their son would be losing her. And he didn't want to lose her. 
So he comes back.

They came back to their home. He was a war hero. Well respected in the community. THey needede to fill their time. SO they bought the land behing their house and started growing vegetables.
He had recovered well from the injuries. BUt he had had some nerve damage in his left leg.
THey still felt a sense of lonliness, and missed their son. But their life was going well. He had his pension to take care of expenses every month. His wife was a local school principal. SO money was never a problem. But they were growing a shit ton of bringals and tomatoes and palak and cucumbers and cabbage, pumpkins. That they started selling those to the local market. 
THe Colonel had been taking medication for his pain for a while. He had looked at various articles online, and undergone physiotherapy. Somebody had suggested that he should go to a psychologist. THe psychologist suggested weed. Not psychologist. Some friend from the army suggested weed. Both of them took weed for the first time. And talked for a bit. THey started laughing for a long time. It was one of those laughs that just has taken some.

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Kid_Mela_Gun_Tea_Hostage.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Being addressed as 'uncle' by a teen boy, held hostage in a dining chair with a steel carnival pellet gun by a girl in Puma runners, while the intruder laughs that his name isn't Preet and the tea turns into sludge
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Flesh out the apartment layout and the smell of the scorching ginger-cardamom tea
> - [ ] Heighten the physical comedy: narrator evaluating whether he can tackle a 14-year-old girl in running shoes
> - [ ] End the scene on the narrator sipping over-boiled bitter kadak chai while watching them raid useless junk

Date: 04-03-2019
Topic: At 5 in the afternoon. (after Frederico Garcia Lorca)

I remember. I heard a knock on the door. I was making tea for myself. My evening tea. It was a boy. 15-16 year old. He wanted to use the toilet. He seemed in a hurry. I let him in. He seemed non threatening. I thought I could handle him. 
He was in there a long time. He really had to go. I readied my list of questions for him: Who are you? All questions seemed to stop there. I wanted to know how he was in the building. He must’ve known someone here to have come in. If he knew someone, then, where is their toilet?
He came out. I just stared at him. Waiting for him to say something about his predicament. 
“Thank you uncle”
Uncle? I’m 33 years old damn it. Yeah. Uncle is probably fine. “No problem. What happened?”
“Nothing uncle. I really had to go to the loo.”
“No. I mean. Who are you?”
“My name is Preet uncle.” He stood there with his hands behind him, making imaginary lines on the floor with his shoes. 
“Where do you live Preet?” I tried hard not to look irritated. 
“C304.”
I should just let him go right? It is too much effort trying to get answers out of this kid. I don’t want to lose my Patience. TEA! I forgot the tea. 
“Okay Preet.” I said “See you around.”
I hurried towards the kitchen. Tea was fine. I cooked it more than I wanted to. But this was fine. Kadak Chai.
I hadn’t heard the door close. Preet was still at the corridor. He was talking to someone outside. 
He saw me and signaled outside. A girl, a little younger than Preet ran in. She had a gun in her hand. Which she pointed at me. 
The girl made me sit down on a chair. Gesturing with her gun. It wasn’t a real gun. It was one of the steel bb guns you get at balloon shooting stands at melas. It wasn’t going to kill me. But I could still hurt me. I didn’t want any extra holes in my body. She was wearing jeans and sweatshirt. White puma running shoes. 
She hadn’t said anything to me. She was just a young kid. I could definitely take her. I was scared. I must have thought about jumping on her at least 10 times. I was too scared to do it. She had this look in her eyes. DO NOT FUCK WITH ME.
Preet was busy running around, filling up his backpack with things. There wasn’t anything valuable in the house. TV was probably the most valuable thing at home. And they couldn’t really take the TV in their backpacks.
“What’s you name?” I asked the girl. She didn’t move. “What’s her name Preet?”
Preet stopped. A backpack in his hand and a grin on his face. “My name isn’t Preet.” And he burst out laughing. Mother Fucker. That annoying laughter when kids laugh at their own stupid jokes.
There was a hint of a smile on the girls face. 
“Can I have my tea at least.” 

***********************************************

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Late_2008_Barber_Shaved_Head_Liberation.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (prose | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Preempting your greatest insecurity by executing it yourself: the cold wind on a newly bald scalp as an act of absolute liberation
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested cold wind on bald scalp and Saurabh's compliment to Voice_and_Dialogue_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Connect to [[The_Runaway_Protagonist_and_the_Hill_Town_Urge]] and [[MOC_02_Escaping_The_Script_Autonomy]]
> - [ ] Flesh out the college cap semester: riding bikes with a baseball cap under the helmet

Date: 09-12-2018
Topic: The first time I wore ______

It was late 2008. I had recently graduated and was waiting for my joining to come. I was about to join one of the biggest IT companies in India. It was my first job. I had graduated as a computer science engineer from a mid level engineering college. 
Early in college — first year in a four year course — I had started losing hair. I was quite conscious about it. I could comb my hair a certain way. I was putting lotions and creams in my hair to grow them back or at east hide the bald spots. In fact I wore a cap for a full semester in college. I would wear it everywhere. In class, going out with friends, even under the helmet while riding the bike. It was ridiculous. I was so insecure about it.
So, in the late 2008, after the college was done and I was waiting, I was losing more hair than I could afford. I had been flirting with the idea of shaving them all off. I thought that will make them grow quicker. Anyway, after thinking about them for a while, I decided to go through with it. I went to the barber and got them shaved. I was so nervous. Didn’t know how will I look.
Coming back from the barber, wind touching my head felt cold. I could see a few people staring. I brushed my hand on my scalp. It felt weird. It felt nice. My hair is gone. This is what I feared most and I had gone and gotten it done myself. I was smiling. I felt so free. No more creams, and oils. No more hiding the bald spot. I don’t have to fear anyone touching my hair. I was free — of all the insecurities. I don’t care if I look good or not. I don’t. This is how I’m going to be from now on. I don’t have to set up my life around my hair routine. Yes, I had a whole routine.
I came home, and my mother had a smile on her face. She thought I looked funny. I didn’t care. I sent a picture to Saurabh, he loved it. He came home to check it out. He laughed as well, but he liked it a lot. A few years later he game me one of the best compliments I’ve ever gotten. “If I looked half as good as you bald, I would do it right away”

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Melting_Solar_System_Box.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (prose | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** A domestic anomaly sitting on a table that alternates between displaying a speeding universe and swallowing pens and matchboxes
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Flesh out character interaction: who brought the box into the house
> - [ ] Connect visually to [[The_One_Hour_God_Communion]] and [[Shadows_Panpsychism_and_Universal_Time]]

Date: 07-01-2019
Topic: Open the box

There is light. Blinding light. I can only look from my peripheral vision at first. As my eyes get used to the light I look inside. A sun is shining. Around it the there are planets rotating. I can’t see the earth at first. I spot a blue ball in the distance. I can see the path these planets are on. Such speed. It is so fast that I can see the trace they are leaving behind them. 
Earth whizzes past me. I close the box. I can’t believe my eyes. I open it again. A full moon, with the bottom covered with clouds. It’s like a paining. I close and open the box again. I see my reflection. IN golden water. Ripples run through my face. It looks like I’m putting my head in the water. My reflection lets a scream out. A huge turbulence in the water. Bubbles everywhere. I don’t know what this means. But it’s scary. I want to know more. But I’m too scared to look. I close the box again. 
I’m not sure what the box wants me to know. It is showing me different things. Space, beauty, me. I don’t know if it makes sense. Does it want to show me my place in the universe. How small I am. Why am I screaming then. Why am I drowning out my own screams. 
I open the box again. It’s empty. There is nothing in there. A minute ago, it had the entire solar system. It wasn’t an image. It was actually there. I could see it clearly. In a distance. The real thing. I pick the box up. There is a pattern carved on the box. A design, which does not mean anything to me. 
I found the box lying at home. I had never seen it before. It was just there. I kept a pen in it. It melted and disappeared into the bottom. I put a matchbox. Same thing happened. I slammed the box shut.

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Michael_Jordan_of_Archery.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine
> **Core Spark:** The irony of a middle-class warrior graduating from an interstate commercial Gurukul (BUSA-Pa-Ga) during a peacetime hiring freeze
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested Eklavya branding lineage gag to Dialogue_and_Voice_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Connected to [[The_Safest_Trail_Why_I_Write]] (institutional education trap) and [[EBOLA_Engineer_Bhai_Dating_Guru_Sketch]]
> - [ ] Map out the master-apprentice dynamic on their first royal hunt
> - [ ] Resolve the two kills mentioned in the hook: the one of necessity and the one by accident

If you really want to hear about it, there are three things you should know about me first. I have only killed two people in my life: One out of necessity and one accidentally. I am ‘the Michael Jordan’ of archery. And I love Mangoes. 

I used to be the lead archer in 5th Archery Battalion of Singhrana Pratapeshwar the third. I was like a normal village kid. My dad used to work for the king, So, we were rich. My father was a finance minister in the king’s court. Although that was not his choice of career. He wanted to be a swordsman or an archer. He wanted to go to battle and return a hero. But my father, as it turned out, was a coward. This of course, was a relief to my grandfather, who wanted his son to be in a safe job. He had been teaching him business and accounts. Pitching projects to the ministers, Lords and Kings. Unfortunately for my father, he was smart. His intelligence meant that he would now have to take over the family business. His dreams stored away in darkness only to be thrust upon his son.

I was neither coward nor smart. Getting into fights all the time, with people much larger than me. My father saw potential in me and put me in Military school. I was not asked and I didn’t mind. And to be honest as a 12 year old I didn’t really know what I was getting into. 
The entire course was a 6 year accelerated leadership program. With boarding, lodging and food included in the course fee. Of course they would make us do most of the work maintaining the school premises. Bhartiya Uch shiksha Acharya Prathmeshwar Gurukul - or BUSAPG for short. Everybody called it BUSA-Pa-Ga for some reason. 
Acharya Prathmesh was some guy who started this gurukul generations ago, when it was A-Pa-Ga. A couple of generations here and there someone had the idea to offer the course for anyone who had money and it became USA-Pa-Ga. Another couple of generations they expanded into other kingdoms offering their course. It needed to have a more widespread appeal. This is the current BUSA-Pa-Ga.

I was an average student. Always showing potential in my teachers eyes, but never quite reaching them. I somehow graduated my 6 years with average marks and started applying for jobs in various battalions in the kingdom. But owing to a prolonged peace in the kingdom, the attrition rate in the armies was almost zero. I had applied to all the major warlords and lieutenants. I had applied to the Lords, but let’s face it, its nothing but a glorified bodyguard job. I was secretly hoping not to get those. Afters months of interviews and applications I decided to take up an internship with a freelance hunter in hopes of learning on the job and honing my skills.

Mahaveer Mrityunjay Lavya was a lazy man. But when Lords and rich people wanted to go hunting without having to deal with their inadequate skills, he was the one they came to. Mahaveer Mrityunjay Lavya once told me that he was from Eklavya’s bloodline. “Ek” he said, “Was the first Lavya. There have been others after him. Dwitiya Lavya, Tritiya lavya”. My father thought it was a marketing stunt. “He is a smart man. If I was in the archery business, I wouldn’t have found a better name to associate with it.” I didn;t care if it was a marketing gimmick, as I saw it, he was the best archer in the Kingdom. Maybe in the top 5 but certainly in the top 10. It was a great chance to learn from a master.

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Shaving an 85,000-rupee synthetic head with a laser scanner; mocking parents who die because they didn't back up to the cloud; waiting on a hill with a busted rifle wishing there was an Assassin's Right Protection Brigade
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**

Date: 02-07-2019
Topic: Something seemed different.

The air was colder. People were looking at me. Turning heads even. I had shaved my head. Have they never seen a bald man before? Is there something on my face. I had checked my newly shaved head in the mirror back at the barbershop. It looked okay. I mean, I wasn’t used to the face yet. It would take a few days still.
I had picked an ordinary head for the shave. I didn’t want to focus on hairstyle, so I went with one without that problem. Hair takes a lot of maintenance. All my previous heads had hair on them. I’ve had four heads in 25 years. My first was the one I came with. From god’s factory. 
My second head was a few days after I turned 14. I had wanted a new head for months now. I had gotten a personal volcano on my forehead. A pimple. I had scratched it and the scar remained. I looked like Harry potter, one of the villains from the 20th and 21st century legends of Lord Voldermort. The teasing TastySchlong_21 and his gang gave me was ridiculous. Mom was against the idea. She said she couldn’t afford it. She had some money saved to get a car. I had called the Children right protection brigade. I got my head in a matter of a few hours. She was still on her first head and didn’t understand what I was going through. Maybe that’s why she looked so - ugly. With her eyes looking in different directions and her oddly shaped head. She said we should be grateful for the body god gave us. She was on her second hand though and both her eyes were new. What did she know.

“What’s the problem… sir” the barber wasn’t sure why I was still standing outside his shop. I wasn’t sure why I was standing there. There were eyes on me. They couldn’t have recognized me though. I showed him the CR code on my arm. He looked at it with his eyes. A small lazer beam recognized the code. “Thanks for your business YourFriend56 sir”. “Don’t call me that,” I shouted. The customers in the shop turned to look at me. “I’m sorry sir. That is your name sir. Isn’t it,” the barber had that ‘what can you do about it’ smile on his face. I hated that. It seemed like everyone had that smile nowadays. Fuck that shit. 

I left without answering. Eighty five thousand rupees at a rundown barbershop. What has the world come to. My dad used to pay only twenty thousand when I was a child. Till he died. Loser. Who dies in this day and age! Loser. That’s what you get for not saving yourself on the cloud. 

************

The party looks great. From 20 miles away at least. Can’t say for sure though. The Z-Ray vision on the scope isn’t working properly. I told my boss to give me a new gun. He said he couldn’t afford it. I wish there was an assassin’s right protection brigade or something. Can’t have everything. I just need to wait for the party to clear. I only need to shoot Business_Tycoon007. Zero zero seven. How did he even get that number. Single digit numbers are too expensive to put on your name. His parents must’ve been rich. You don’t get 001 or 002s anymore. Anything under 20 is almost impossible to get. I had to pay 
What’s th

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Rispana_Bridge_Floods_and_Black_Cat_Curfew.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (prose | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Viewing civil unrest and flash floods through the naive lens of a child happy to miss three months of school
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Link to regional fiction projects and coming-of-age stories
> - [ ] Expand the Rampur Tiraha agitation context and the bridge commando beat

Date: 21-12-2018
Topic: Write about a river

There is a river a few hundred meters in front of my house. It’s a known landmark across the city. The bridge that connects my house to the rest of the city is on the river. Rispana bridge over the Rispana river. It is a strange name for a river though. I’ve never noticed it. Rispana. Ris-pana. Risp-ana. What would it mean. I don’t know a word that would mean anything.
The river is dry. At least most times of the year. During monsoon it fills up. About 24-25 years ago, there was a flood in the river as well. The area was not as populated as it is today. There are many more concrete houses and buildings. In those days there were a few tin huts. Maybe a few bricks to support the tin roof. Most of these houses did not survive the gushing waters of the river. 
Most of the houses were on the river banks. Maybe a few yards away from the river. The ground on which the houses were built were the kind that could be damaged easily. Our house was built at a safe distance. We could see from our terrace, what the river was doing to the banks. Eroding the entire area quickly with a bull’s rage. We saw the river take people with it. Houses melted in it, taken away to some other place. 
Since then, the river banks have been rebuilt. Made up of hard concrete with a strong base. People have built their houses away from the river. I don’t think we will see a similar incident again. 
Rispana bridge has gone through its ups and downs. It is a witness to millions of stories. We have been witnesses together in a few of them. I’ve been a part of its stories since it was a small bridge that could take only a few vehicles across. Then it broke one day. It remained broken for months, maybe a couple of years. Finally rebuilt — made wider, stronger. Now it looks well dressed ready to go to work. 
There was a time a couple of decades ago, when Dehradun was in a curfew. On and off for about 3 months. It happened after a peaceful march towards Delhi, demanding a new state, was shot at by the police in a place called Rampur in Mujjafarnagar.  The incident was named ‘Rampur tiraha kaand’. Subsequently there was a curfew across the city. Anything that required going out on the road was illegal. Rispana bridge was the one of the main ways get in and out of the city. The state government posted ‘Black cat commandos’ on the bridge. They would catch trying to pass the bridge. People going to buy food, people going to hospitals. They would either beat the person caught taking the stroll or they would beat them and put them in jail for a couple of nights. I witnessed many of these incidents from my terrace. It was entertaining in a way. I was a small kid. I didn’t really know what it all really meant. I was happy with not going to school for 3 months.

---

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Signboard_Painter.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (Destination 3)
> **Core Spark:** A satirical inversion of artistic rebellion: an artist shunned as a "laborer" by a pedigree family of intellectual poets.
> 
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Fix prosodic inconsistencies: replace uneven rhyming couplets with structured blank verse or disciplined free verse.
> - [ ] Sharpen the father's dialogue regarding the "intellectual art" of literature vs. the physical mess of paint.
> - [ ] Keep the ending rationalization: the rebel artist immediately compromising by looking for commercial commissions.

Write a character description or a poem about a person based on you, one struggling to create some type of art but who is bound by family obligations. When you finish, ask yourself how you feel about this person. Are you sympathetic to his struggle? Then ask yourself of you extend such sympathy to yourself.

They shone in the sky, my father and his father before him.
They were poets but there was poetry written on them.
Absolute legends in their field. 
They expected me to write as well, and in my age become a poet of the big screen.

But my mind is in colors, violet, orange and magenta,
I love how they come together in a sunset, Its as if god is a painter.
I want to mix them too, there is a lot I can create,
I want to become god with a few colors on my plate.

I can see the disappointment in my mother’s eyes,
My fathers eyes too if he were here, 
uneducated, idiot he would have said. Writing is an intellectual art. 
I wanted you to be praised. Paint beautiful pictures with a phrase.
Here you are doing a laborer’s job. 
Painting? What are you going to do paint sign boards?
If he were here.

But I can see it in my mother’s eyes. 
It’s as if my dad is taking through them.
I’ll show them on the canvas. I can show them what I can do.
What I want to do. What I’m meant to do.
What I love. What I’ll give my life for.
If they can’t see that, then that is on them.
I can’t live on their whim.

Now what do I do, what do I put on this canvas.
What do I make that makes people notice.
A portrait? How about the sunset? An object? I can’t decide.
Everyone makes these things. What will separate me from them.
What will make me famous? What will make me rich?
I can do commissions. You know till I figure stuff out. It’ll only be on the side.

I’m not selling out. It’s what everyone has to do.
It’s a journey I’m taking. I’ll do it for a few bucks for the time.
I’ll do it for myself when I know what I want to create.
Violet, orange and magenta, I want to conjure my own sunset.
Or maybe worlds that no one has ever seen. I want to be the god of my own canvas.
Can’t wait!


================================================================================
DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Slam_Book_Friends_and_Rajkumars_Dirty_Books.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Teaming up with quiet Nepali boy Abhinav to make birthday cards and slam books while drowning out bad-boy Rajkumar reading dirty books aloud; reconnecting on Facebook decades later as muted strangers
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Flesh out the GRD Academy hostel atmosphere in Dehradun
> - [ ] Contrast childhood intimacy (making cards and slam books) with adult social media detachment

Date: 31-03-2019
Topic: "So, these were my friends . . ." (after John Balaban)

There was a boy, Abhinav, in my class in the boarding school. He was Nepali; from Kathmandu. I was into sports — cricket, football, basketball. So, most of the friends I made in school were kids who played some sports. Abhinav didn’t. We still became friends. He was a quite, shy guy. Got along with everybody. We became friends because even I was quite and shy in those days. I think I still am. 
We used to hang out a lot. All the kids around her were boisterous and loud. There was an abuse in every sentence they spoke. All except me and Abhinav. We sat in class together. Used to talk about music and films. We teamed up for class quizzes. We made cards together for people on their birthdays and other festivals. We used to make slam books for other people. It was easy. I don’t remember doing much else with him. He used to be my best friend back then. 
There was another kid called Rajkumar, who was infamous for being a bad boy. He used to bring dirty books in our class and read them. Abhinav and I were innocent. Horrified by the sexual nature of the stories, we used to put out fingers in our ears and try to drown out the narration. It was funny, now that I think about it. I think the stories were to get a reaction out of us, rather than do everybody else a service. 
I lost touch with him when I left GRD. Back then there weren’t many ways to keep in touch. You can exchange phone numbers. But that didn’t occur to either one of us. I don’t know why. We did like each other. We would have been friends given any other environment. I would’ve like to still be friends with him. 
I got in touch with him a few years ago on Facebook. Added him as a friend. But we did’t chat much. I didn’t know what to talk to him about. He probably felt the same. Our personalities got in the way. I was never used to making small talks and catching up. He was like me so I’ll assume it was the same for him. It had been so long that we are strangers to each other now. I’ve definitely changed a lot. He must have as well. 
I went to another school after I left GRD. I lived at home now. Going to a new school, and meeting new people was nerve wracking. I sat next to this kid Deepank. He was a strange kid. But all the kids were strange. We became friends. He had a twin brother who was a bit more sensible. 
Deepank was a romantic. I don’t know how to talk about him. He liked a girl but didn’t know what the protocol was to convey those feelings. So he used to get her gifts. Say nice things. THe girl knew. Another kid told her. She was surprised by that. Which is strange because you have to be either stupid to not notice or lying. 
I’m not in touch with him anymore. BUt he is still in my life in a way. My friends and I make fun of the things he posts on his facebook page. It’s overtly romantic shit, cheesy to be exact. It’s a source of amusement for us. And an ice breaker when conversations stop in our chat group. I wonder what would he say if he knew his role in our lives. 
I feel I should talk to him sometime. I feel I should talk to Abhinav as well. I would like to know how both these people turned out. Who are they now? I hope someday I will. Why is it hard to reconnect with people. Is it like that only for me? I hope so. It would be a shame if people don’t. There are so many things that are taking us away from each other. From people we like. Or liked at some point in our lives. Shame shouldn’t be another reason.

---

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)

How does my story begin?
WHat is the initial experience?
What images and ideas emerge?

THe story begins with a girl going about her life. SHe is frustrated with her life. SHe doesn't know what to do. SHe doesn't feel passionaltely about her job. SHe is in a relationship where she is not in love. We need to start the story right in the middle of all these problems. Another way to approach this could be to show her getting into this trap. But that would be too long wouldn't it. It'll be like going through all her mistakes. Not mistakes but like, life choices. WE can do that in a montage. Because we need to get to the point of the story. 
Maybe we could show her getting a ciggarette. I see her smoking everyday. But not because she likes it so much. SHe hates smoking. But it is a way to get away from her office and boss. SHe is trying to not think about work. BUt that is what she always does. SHe thinks about work while smoking. SHe is now addicted. I also see her talkign to her boss. With her head bowed down. Trying to fake confidence. Speaking little. ANd generally nodding. SHe tries to put her points forward. Which are valid. But htey are shut down and she doesn't stand up for them. I see her trying to get away fro her boss. I see her trying to escape his glances and looks because she doens;t want to hear another lecture.
THe initial eperience is of frustration. Its stuffy. Everything is closing down. It's like you are in an elevator and there are people entering. First there is just one. THen another. And suddenly a family enters. Then another family. Your hands are stuck to your side. You are just trying to look at the elevator cieling because you want to breathe. YOu can't move. And another person enters. And you feel like you'll die of suffocation. You can't move your hands and legs and you're at the back of the elevator. Even if it opens it'll be difficult getting out. You want to get out. You feel like you'll die and the only thing that can save you is the elevator door opening. You've decided that you'll get out no matter which floor it opens on. But it never opens.

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Terrace_Cigarette_Romantic_Heist.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (prose | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** A painstakingly engineered romantic rooftop getaway completely derailed when the person walking through the door is just your buddy asking for a smoke
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested terrace getaway and Roy's anti-climactic entrance to Voice_and_Dialogue_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Connect to [[EBOLA_Engineer_Bhai_Dating_Guru_Sketch]] and [[The_Five_Minute_Mallu_Wedding_Monologue]]
> - [ ] Sharpen the internal dialogue analyzing non-verbal party signals

Date: 23-11-2018
Topic: It’s Saturday afternoon. You’re not at home

I had too much to drink last night. Couldn’t make myself call a cab and go home. I was so stoned. Dangerous combination. Alcohol, weed and good company. Listened to music till late last night. 
There is this time at a party. You’re drunk. Sleep seems like a good option so you want to go home. But there are people still there at the party. They’re having fun — you too. So you stay. There is a girl at the party. She is beautiful. Especially right now. Kissing her would be the best thing right now. But there are just so may people here. And I don’t know if she would want to kiss me back. So you just think of ways to find out. I sit close to her. See her reactions. Is she getting closer to me? Is she leaning her head on my shoulders? Is she touching my arm. Where does she look when I talk to her. She is looking right in my eyes. Maybe she wants it too. Not that looking into my eyes is any kind of indication. It’s just good manners. But I see something else in her eyes. Maybe she wants me to make a move. I can’t it’s too risky. I don’t want to be the sleazy guy. I don’t want to make her uncomfortable. BUt It’s more about me than her. I care about what she would think about me. 
I shouldn’t give a fuck. Right? Does it really matter? I can ask her. When everyone is gone. But so many people are still here. It doesn’t look like they want to go yet. What should I do? I go to the terrace. Alone. With a cigarette and another drink. Hoping that she will follow me. Give me company. That’ll be the perfect signal I was looking for. But even then. I can’t be sure. Should I ask her? Look for other signals? I should just ask her right? What is the romance in that. Hey Tanisha, I really feel like kissing you. That doesn’t sound so bad. Maybe I can make it a bit romantic. I can hold her hand, and ask. Maybe. 
With this plan in mind, I go to the roof. Drink in hand, cigarette on my lips and lighter in my pocket. I look at her. Trying to show her where I’m going. Not explicitly, just by eye contact. She is talking to someone. Someone asks me “Where are you going?” I show them the cigarette. THey nod their head.
There is a nice breeze on the roof. I keep my glass on the ledge. Light my cigarette. I’m thinking of things to talk about when she is here. I don’t want to stand awkwardly not having anything to say. I want her to think I’m interesting. 
I’ve smoked half the cigarette. I don’t think she’ll come. I’m staring at the entrance. Suddenly I hear footsteps coming up. I turn around. Don’t want her to think I was waiting for her. Be chill. “Dude, can I share that cigarette with you?” It’s Roy. “Sure.”
I come down 5 minutes later. Pick up my stuff, call a cab.

DOCUMENT: Short_Stories/The_Wall_Balcony_and_3AM_Mediator.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** A concrete wall amplifying a 3 AM marital cross-examination where the husband starts aggressive, shrinks as evidence mounts, and demands his own female friend mediate
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Compress timeline into a single 3 AM night on the wall balcony
> - [ ] Frame narrator pulling black garbage-bag hood off the weed plant while listening to the acoustic echo trial
> - [ ] Build up to husband's pivot from loud bluster to proposing a female friend as 'neutral mediator'

Date: 01-03-2019
Topic: Write about a balcony

The view wasn’t great. There was a building right on front of it. It was shaped like a wall. Just one big wall going all the way across my balcony. A view of people’s laundry being dried. Sometimes you would hear someone flushing down their deeds. I hated it. I would take an open field or a pool of garbage spread across a canal any day over this block of concrete to block my air.
Looking back at it, I wish there were stories I could tell about that balcony. I used to sit there sometimes. It wasn’t a soul cleanser, but it felt nice. Sometimes I would sit with Pooja. Listening to coke studio or Paolo Nutini. Sometimes I would play my guitar for her — even when she stopped paying attention to me; even when she was checking her Instagram feed; even when she was deep in her thoughts that may or may not have me in them — I was playing for her. I was trying to impress her. I still am. Everyday. 
I had some plants there. There was a couple of money plants that grew to a decent size. There was some kind of flower on the ledge that my roommate and I shared. I had tried growing weed in a big pot — no pun intended — a couple of times. 
The first time it grew like I hoped it would. It had buds like I thought new buds should be like. I screwed up during the flowering. The weed plant is supposed to get at least 12 hours of darkness. I kept it inside my room, thinking it would get at least 12 hours of darkness in the room for sure. I forgot it needed proper sunlight during the other 10-12 hours as well. And also, people in my house forgot to water it when I was away.
The second time the growth wasn’t as dense as the first time. But I had a bog strong plant. There were good buds after 3-4 months. For flowering, I had made a big plastic sheet out of shopping bags. I had doubled it up with a black garbage back to block all sunlight when the plant was under it. I would put it on everyday in the evening when the sun was about to go down. I would pull it up first thing in the morning — literally as I was waking up. Didn’t work. I don’t know what happened. I didn’t get any thick flowers that I could smoke. Maybe it was the seed. Maybe it was what I did. I didn’t get a chance to experiment more with it.
There was something interesting that happened one night. It’s about 3 AM. I’m trying to sleep - which means I had been watching youtube videos for at least an hour. I hear loud shouting noises. My first thought was, somebody’s television is too loud. But, immediately I realized it could be domestic violence. So I got up with a jerk, went to my balcony to listen.
A woman was crying and shouting at a man. It was in the other building. One that is shaped like a wall. It was loud. Anything said in the middle of the night echoed between our buildings anyway. But this was actual shouting. The woman had been suspicious of the man, who was her husband. She had been observing his interactions with one specific girl, over the past few months. She had seen his messages this time, and there were some things in there she didn’t approve of.
The husband got a few chances to tell his side as well. Initially he took an aggressive approach. Denying everything, and blaming the wife for even suspecting him. He was loud in this confident phase of the fight. As the story unfolded, and the wife put forth some solid arguments, he got quieter. Now he really had to think about his answers. 
I followed the fight till it’s end. It went on for a couple of hours at least. There was no resolution. The husband didn’t accept the allegations. The wife did not concede that there wasn’t anything wrong. They decided to call in a mediator. A girl friend of the guy. 
I still remember that night. I was sitting on the balcony the whole time. Listening in. I know it’s rude to eavesdrop. But it’s not like I had to make any special efforts. Even if I was in my room — on my bed, with a blanket over my head — I could hear it. Certain words weren’t clear, which is why I moved to the balcony, but it was booming. It was entertaining. I wasn’t harming anyone. My listening to them had absolutely no effect on their marital life.

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine (short_story | 2018_Crucible)
> **Core Spark:** Lions and elephants negotiating deals like corporate diplomats; a fragile centuries-old peace where predators obey non-aggression zones simply because breaking them brings mutual inconvenience
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Frame the stream treaty as an allegory for modern international treaties or corporate detente
> - [ ] Sharpen the dialogue among the birds reporting rumors of the great beasts

Date: 12-06-2019
Topic: Just beyond the edge of woods

There is a stream. And in that stream animals gather to quench their thirsts. Deer and buffalo. Elephant and giraffe. Lions and wolves. They drink their water and leave. Some of them talk to each other. They tell each other of the dangers in the forest. How the lions try to kill some deer’s best friend. And how that has left the poor guy in trauma. He now hunts only on the inside of the herd. He doesn’t get to eat much there. Most of the grass grazed by the deers gathered before. 
Nobody talks to the lions. They talk to you. Except the elephants. Nobody knows what they talk about. From a distance it looks very official. Like officers from two separate department negotiating a deal. Elder animals say, it is impossible for us to guess what they talk about. They are beasts of power. And powerful beasts have a strange relationship. It comes from somewhere between fear and respect. Some birds say they’ve heard them talk about the great beast. But then again. You ccan’t believe the birds. They say anything. THe greatest beasts this forest has seen are the elephants.
All animals know the lions won’t attack near the stream. A deal had been reached hundreds of years ago etween all the animals in the eoods. Lake and the stream are safe zones. If an animal, such as the lion was to break the deal. He would not be allowed to drink water from the stream. All the animals will gang up on him and drive him away. THere had been an example of a family of hyneas killed by the elephants and the buffalos. It’s a flimsy deal. One that the these cornivores can break easily. THey can get their water from any part of the stream where there are fewer animals. It’s not guarded by animals everywhere on it’s path. Or they can drink during the night. When most animals keep away. But it becomes awfully inconvenient. Most predators didn’t have any shortage of food. So they didn’t mind this minor inconvenience.

How I will commit a murder.

I�m a non violent person. But when I watch these murder mysteries on TV. Or read about them in newspapers. I can�t help but feel that the killers are so dumb to get caught. Apart from a few true crime stories where the killers were never found or the murder was never proven to be done by the main suspect. I used to think committing a murder and getting away with is so easy. I was thinking about this not too long ago. But now that I�ve gotten down to writing about it, I�m not so sure. Nevertheless, I�ll give it a go.
Let�s start with just committing a murder where the point is to commit the murder and not get caught. Now the victim can be anyone. A stanger picked at random, someone I know, someone who knows me. Doesn�t matter. The point is to get away with it. But in all three cases the way of doing it will be different I think. Someone I know, and someone who knows me puts me in the suspect list straight away. So it ought to be different. So let�s start with a random stranger.
First of all I�ll acknowledge the fact that this comes off as a bit psychopathic. Killing a random stranger. With no motive but to prove to myself that it can be done without getting caught. So I�m just thinking about it. I�m not going to do it in real life. I just want to make that clear. 
SO I�ll have to pick someone first. I�ll probably pick someone on the street. Far away from the area I live in. So Let�s say I�m in Bangalore. I go to Whitefield. THe problem with that is I�m not familiar with whitefield. This will go against me. I�ll have to plan a few things in advance. Where am I going to do it? How will I do it? And What will I do with the body.
Where am I going to do it? I can�t do it in a public place. I can�t do it with people around. I don�t want to leave witnesses. They can recognize me. So first of all I have to make myself unrecognizable. Ill have to get some makeup done. I�ll have to do something that if and when somebody sees me. THey give the police a description that is as far from me as possible. So, since I�m bald, I�ll put some kind of wig. I�ll also have to wear spectacles. Thick framed. That changes the face a little bit. I need to change the shape of my face as well. So,I will need one of those dental prosthetics. I don�t know where I will find it. I think I can. But why attract attention. I can probably buy a lot of chewing gums and put them in my mouth. To change the shape of my jaw and cheeks. WHat about beard. Do I need a beard? Maybe. I�ll grow my beard for a few days and on the day I�m going to do it. I can shave rest of the beard and just keep the mustache. I need to do something about he hair. 
This can be a great story. About a writer who commits a murder researching about murder. Should I stop now. Wy amI feeling liek I should stop. I�m getting this voice inside me that says I should stop and do this one when I have more time. I have time now. What is the problem. Okay I�ll listen to the voice. I�ll stop for now. I�ll do this once I�m done with the assignment tomorrow.

So the writer is doing some kind of research on committing murder.I�ll prbably coninue with this. I�ll have to integrate what I�ve written today.

Write a scene that is set at night. It should posses an element of intrigue or mystery. Write this scene at night. Push yourself to take risks with the language or the images or simple withe events in the scene. Allow the scene to end without resolution so that at least one more scene will be needed to provided some sort of closure. Write that scene in the morning. Put the scene away for a few days, then take them out and read them. Do they connect? Do the actions and the tone match?

He checked his watch once again. 10:34 it said. He looked through the scope again. It had been almost an hour now that an car had come on the road. This seemed unusual to him. It was the highway. And although it was far from the city, there was no shortage of travelers at night. He looked through his binoculars. He couldn’t see anything approaching for miles. The watch showed 10.37.
His hand went to his pocket and took out a pouch. It had pre-rolled joints inside. He sat back on the tree putting the tree putting the joint between his lips. 
“Hey Doc”
“Hi” said the voice from the other side of the call.
“Doc, I’ve been feeling that anxiety again” stressing on anxiety, air-quoting with one hand. “And I’m about to smoke a joint. Like you said.” He waited for a response from the other end. There was none. “I’m not sure if this is a good idea. It makes me slow.”
“That’s the idea Samir” the voice said. “It is supposed to calm you down. So that you don’t get… thoughts.”
Samir knew he didn’t have to smoke if he wanted. It was only advice, not an order. But he wanted to try whatever was necessary.
“Sure doc. If you…” He saw something from the corner of his eye. He picked up the binoculars. There was a car in a distance. 4 maybe 5 kms out. 
He got into position. 
“You there? Samir…” the voice said.
“Yeah doc. Sorry. I got distracted. I… uh… I’ll call you tomorrow.”
“Listen Samir, its okay if you don’t want to do it. We can talk about it if you want. I’m sure even that helps”
His brain was now calculating all the scenarios. His heart was racing. He had a smile on his lips. This is waht he was waiting an hour for. The car will take about 4 minutes to come in my range. The wind is about 5 kms per hour. His training was kicking in.
“No doc. Okay here I’m lighting it now.” He lit the joint and took a puff. He coughed as he took his first drag.
“Haha. You’ll get used to it. Don’t worry. We can still talk if you…”
“Doc. I’ll call you tomorrow.” He hung up.
He took another drag. This time it went down smoothly. He took another and another. 
He had his eyes on the target. The car was still 3 minutes away. His finger was on the trigger. He had already calculated all the variables. He knew the exact position the car needed to be when he pulled the trigger. Now he needed to clear his mind and focus on the task.
2 minutes.
Maruti zen. White number plate. Some poor middle class guy. Does he have a family? 
Wait. No. Clear your mind. Breathe.
In. Out. In. Out.
Why do I care if he has a family. I don’t know him. Or his family. Thousands of people die everyday. Should I care about everyone’s family.
Samir. Don’t do this. In. Out.
1 minute.
What if I miss. What if he escapes. From this distance it’ll be impossible to miss. I already know what I have to do. I’ve set everything up. All I need to do is pull the trigger at the right mom.. Damn it. Why can’t I clear my head. 
30 seconds
Should I aim for the wheels. I wonder what happens if I don’t kill him. Will I still feel the need? Do I feel the need right now? I don’t feel excited. Samir. You’ve come all this way. It’s just another random person. You can’t think about these things now. It’s too late. It’s too late. IT’S TOO LATE

He pulled the trigger. A loud noise filled the highway. The car swerved and hit a milestone.
Samir sat up on his back. Took a deep breath. “Oh fuck!”

*****************

```fountain
EXT. LIBRARY. - DAY

Vartika(F,28) is browsing the shelf looking at books. She takes out a book and leafs through it. Diya(F,28) comes from behind and taps her on the shoulder.
      DIYA
(Whispering)
Did you find it?
Vartika closes the book and keeps it back
VARTIKA
(whispering)
Not sure we'll find anything here. (Drop the first word)
Vartika continues to look, going up and down the shelf.
DIYA
Why don't we just ask the librarian?
VARTIKA
(mumbling to herself, reading book titles)
Time dilation, Through the wormhole, temporal paradox...
DIYA
This is taking too much time
Vartika stops. 
VARTIKA
(Points to the librarian with her eyes)
Don't want him to know what we're looking for... Or anyone else.
 (Delayed Answer)
Vartika gets back to the books. Diya is still staring at the librarian.
DIYA
My stepdad is a librarian. Broke his foot once. In the Library. Can you imagine? Had to roll around in a wheelchair for a...(pauses) (Tangent)
Diya remembers something and puts her hand on her forehead.
VARTIKA
What?
DIYA
(Loud)Stephen Hawking... (Yelling) He's alive right now...
LIBRARIAN (O.S.)
(Yelling)
SILLENNNCCEEEE. (External Interruption)
DIYA
(whispering)
... and he lives here at Cambridge.
Vartika stops browsing, turns around to face Diya.
VARTIKA
Date today? (Sentence Fragment)
Diya hurriedly looks for something in her bag. She takes out a device that looks like a bracelet. It has multiple dials. She looks at one of the dials and presses a button.
DIYA
13th March.. 
Vartika has a realization.
VARTIKA
Quick. Grab your stuff. We need to hurry.
DIYA
What? Why?
VARTIKA
His health is... We only have... 
(Takes a deep breath)
He dies tomorrow. (Starts again)
Diya raises her eyebrows in shock. They run out of the library as fast as they can.
```

On a road. Somewhat busy street.

Vlogger (Nerdy boy. Glasses.): Hey guys, swagat hai aapka mere channel Engineer Bhai on Love and Attraction (EBOLA) me aur mai hun aapka dost aapka adviser engineer bhai.
Doston mai aap logon ko request karunga ki please comments me ma behen ki gaaliyan na likhen. Meri ma behen  bhi mere videos dekhti hain. Par koi nahi inme se kayi comments mere doston ke hain. *Grinding his teeth* unko to mai dekh lunga. Sunny to ruk, tujhe tere hostel me dekhta hun…
*Composing himself* To doston, mere kayi followers ne apne success stories bheji hain. Wo mai aapke saath aj share karna chahta hun.
*Picks up a piece of paper* First is, Arjun Khatri from Wadgaon Academia of Science and Technical Education. 3rd year, Mechanical student. *Stares at the paper**Slowly* Wadgaon Academia of Science and Technical Education… *Pauses**Chuckles* Waste… hahaha waste college…Hahaha. 
*Composes himself again* Sorry. To Arjun Khatri from Waste college likhte hain ki inki branch me ek ladki hai. *Looking at the camera with a sympathetic face* Mechanical ki tragedy.
Arjun Bhai ko ye ladki first year se pasand hai. Mera video “Ladki patane ke liye unse baat karni padti hai” dekha. Mai video ka link yahan de raha hun.*Points up but the video link comes on his face*
Doston mera video Arjun Bhai ne second year me dekha aur finally 3rd year me himmat juta ke us ladki ko hi bola, aur sabse khushi ki baat ye ki ladki ne wapas hi bola bhai ko. *Pause* 
*Puts the paper under his armpit and claps* Bahut badhiya Arjun bhai, girlfriend banane ki disha me ye aapka pehla kadam tha. Bahut khushi hui *Wipes fake tears from his eyes* hamare ek chote bhai ne aj apni hone waali girlfriend se pehli baar baat ki. Congrats.
Hamari second success story hai *Hesitates* 
Cameraman (We can’t see him. We see the camera shaking): Abey bol na.
Vlogger: Hamari second success story hai… *Hesitates again*
Cameraman: Bol na bhenchod. Khada kar diya hai idhar subah se.
Vlogger: Bas yahi thi. Aur koi success story nahi hai. 
*A slipper comes flying and hits the vlogger. He straightens himself up*
Vlogger: Hamari second success story hai… *Grinding his teeth* *Mumbles* First year ke Rajat Arora ki…
Cameraman: Abey dhang se bol na…
Vlogger: Abey kya dhang se… Saale first year me ladki patane chale hain… Yahan logon chautha saal repeat kar rahe hain ki is saal ek pategi *Boldly looking into the camera* Inke senior kya hath me drafter leke baithe hue hain. 
*looks at the paper* Mumbai and Saurashtra technical institute *Stares* MASTI??!! Kya bole bhenchod. *Looks at the camera and remembers he is still shooting*
*To the cameraman* Ye waala part edit kar ke upload karna please. 
*A thumb comes up to the camera, giving thumbs up to the Vlogger*
*Cut: A black screen with words “Ooops”

Vlogger (To the cameraman) *Hassled but calms himself down*: continue karen?
*Camera goes up and down*
Vlogger: To guys, Maine techniques aur strategy pe aapko kaafi theory di hai. Aj time hai Practical lesson ka. 
Ham khade hain Brijmohan Indumati technological college and hostel ke saamne.

---

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine
> **Core Spark:** Inversion of Indian parental marriage pressure: moms protesting incompetent 20-somethings getting married too young
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [ ] Wrap the sketch in Fountain formatting for clean scene/dialogue breaks
> - [ ] Add more field-interview beats with placards and crowd reactions
> - [ ] Contrast the LinkedIn/Tinder confusion with traditional matrimonial dynamics

Thousands of parents have come together for a rally. Most of them are moms of boys and girls around 21-25.

They are standing in front of the parliament house. Asking the lawmakers of our country to update our country’s marriage laws. 
Let’s go talk to one of the protesters. 

Aapka naam?
Ji Mrs Upadhyay. (fixes her placard. Its says “Azaadi”)
Kya maange hain aapki Mrs Upadhyay?
Ji wo hum, Marriage ki leagal age badhana chahte hain.
Legal age?
Ji
Aapko nahi lagta ki jo current legal age hai, 18 aur 21 ki wo kaafi hai.
Nahi ji. Mera beta abhi 24 ka ho gaya hai use abhi omellete banana bhi nahi aata. APne kapde fold kar ke nahi rakhta. Kamra hamesha ganda. Ab aap hi bataiye, kya aisa insaan shaadi karne layak hai?
Lekin, ye to aap par hai na ki aap uski shaadi kab karayen.
Wo meri sunta kahan hai. Bolta hai job milte hi shaadi kar lega. 
To fir wo job dhoondh raha hai?
Haan pata nahi mobile se dhoondh raha hai. Pata nahi Job dhoondh raha hai ya ladki.
Matlab?
Tinder aur Linked in dono hain uske paas. Pata nahi kya karta hai.
To aap kya chahti hain, Legal age kya honi chahiye?
Kam se kam 30. 
30!!??
Ji. Mujhe lagta hai perfect age hai.
Okay (Surprised) Dhanyawad Mrs Upadhyay. Wish you all the best.

Ab ham chalte hain Mr and Mrs Parmar ke paas, jinki 27 saal ki beti ne apne liye ladka pasand kar liya hai, aur isi saal shaadi karne ka plan bana rahi hai. Aiiye.

Namaskar Mr and Mrs Parmar. (Mr Parmar wipes a single tear from his eye with his sleeve)
-Both- Namaste
Kya apko nahi lagta ki aapki beti shaadi ki umar ki ho gayi hai?
-Mrs- She’s only 27 (disgusted by the question)
(Surprised. Looks around to see if he is hearing correctly.) Yes. (beat) Exactly. Twenty seven. 
-Mr- Sir, Hamari beti hai sattais saal ki hai. Uska boyfriend hai do saal se. Wo usse shaadi karne…

-Mrs- Abhi time hi kitna hua hai date karte hue. Ye bacche samajhte hi nahi.
Lekin

================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine
> **Core Spark:** The visual grammar of a high-stakes communal protest mapped onto standard corporate appraisal complaints
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested manager protest interview dialogue to Dialogue_and_Voice_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Format in Fountain syntax with clear scene headings and dialogue cues
> - [ ] Connected to [[Parents_March_Azaadi_Sketch]] and [[The_Whitefield_Murder_Research]]
> - [ ] Draft Act 2: The HR department arriving with riot police to negotiate Sudhir's severance

There are 15-10 people gathered in front of an apartment building. 
There are burning effigies and posters of a man.
THey are shouting slogans. “Sudhir Kumar haye haye”, “Sudhir kumar haye haye”
A reporter on the screen.

Ham khade hain Social Media Manager Sudhir Kumar ki society ke bahar.
Sudhir Kumar kaam karte hain, Dilli ki ek mamuli company me.

Yahan khade hain unki company ke kuch karmachari. 
Aayiye ham unse baat karte hain.

A crowd is aggressively chanting. She signals a man from the crowd — he is shouting slogans with both hands raised and shaking violently —  to come talk to her. 
His t shirt has come up to his chest. Hair standing. He has a slipper in his hand. He stops. Fixes his hair with his slipper. Pulls his T- shirt down. He looks behind to see if she is talking to someone else. Points to himself and nods. She waves again.

He walks up to her. 

Kya naam hai apka?
Ji Sameer.
Sameer ji. Aap Shudir Kumar ko kaise jaante hain?
Ji Manager hain hum uske.
Mhhmmmmmhhmmm. Sameer ji Kya shikayaten hain aapko Sudhir kumar ji se.

Madam, Ek to, he fails to deliver results on time. Dusra, his communication with teammates is unsatisfactory. And third, Initiative to bilkul hi nahi leta. HE IS AN IDIOT.

The reporter nods seriously, looks at the camera and says “To dekh sakte hain aap. Sudhir kumar ke manager ki jubani. Unka kehna hai ki wo ek Number ke nalayk the.

Aayie ek aur Sudhir Kumar ke naraz kolleegz(Colleagues) se baat karte hain.

Aapka naam.
Guy smiles. Looks at the camera nervously. Parkas.
Prakash ji. Aaap Sudhir kumar ko kaise jaante hain.

Prakash smiles and looks at the camera nervously. Ji hum Jhaadu lagate hai aafis(office) me.

Jhaadu?
Prakash smiles and looks at the camera again. Ji.

The reporter raises her eyebrows. Jhaadu?

Prakash smiles and looks at the camera. The reporter pushes him away.

To jaisa ki aap dekh sakte hain Sudhir Kuma

DOCUMENT: Sketches_and_Monologues/The_Better_Lie_Election_Monologue.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine
> **Core Spark:** Voting framed not as ideological choice, but as boredom with old political lies and a gamble on new ones
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested 'better jhooth' voting routine to Dialogue_and_Voice_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Connected to [[Parents_March_Azaadi_Sketch]] (political and civic satire)
> - [ ] Polish the progression: Education lie -> Development lie -> National danger lie

Election khatam hue abhi. Maine vote nahi diya. I feel aap log mujhe judge kar rahe ho. Dekho mere saath around 36 percent logon ne bhi nahi kiya. Mai minority me hun. Mujhe samajh hi nahi aata kise vote karun. Bahut difficult choice hai. Har koi hamse jhooth bolta hai. Har koi. Tum kisi bhi neta ko utha ke uska purana record dekh lo. I guarantee usne junta ko jhoothe waade jhoothi tassaliyan di hongi. 

At this point hum vote karke ye decide kar rahe hain ki better jhooth kaun bolta hai.Ek bolega hum education best kar denge, dusra bolega hum development best kar denge. Hum soch rahe hain. Yaar education waala jhooth to bahut ho gaya. Ye development waala ekdum naya hai. Pata nahi jhooth hai bhi ki nahi? Real nikla to? Kahin kar hi na de development. CHalo decided. Is baar isko vote karte hain. 

5 saal ho jaate hain. Kuch hota nahi. Is beech aapko dono side jhooth bolti hain. Ek kehti hai kuch nahi kiya is sarkar ne. Sarkar bolti hai dekho ye road banayi to. Ye dekho ye statue banayi to. Aur tumhe lagta hai haan yaar. Meri road to nahi bani. Lekin chalo koi to bani hai. 
Fir election aa jaate hain. Sarkar fir junta ke saamne. Hum development karenge. Junta: Kahan sir. Ye waala jhooth to ho gaya. Koi aur sunao. Sarkar: *Thinking* okay okay… how about this. Musalmano se hame khatra hai. Junta: Arey sir, wo to ho gaya na. Kashimir gujarat maratha sab jagah. Sarkar: Umm.. Nahi nahi. Is baar poore desh ko khatra hai. Junta: Oo bete ki aisa kya. Sir ye waala mast hai. Chalo ote aapko diya.

TO matlab ye jhooth bol raha tha!! PAr last time to usko diya tha. Last time to wo jhooth bol raha tha. Oooohhh… Matlab sab hooth bolte hain. Oh my god. Ye to dimaag fat gaya mera.


But at this point I feel hum loog decide kar rahe hain, ki zyada acha jhooth kaun bolta hai. Dekho corrupt to sabhi hai. Kaam koi karta nahi. Bolte sab hai ki ham kuch to karenge. Lekin karta koi nahi. To is point pe hame ye to realize kar hi lena chahiye ki sab jhooth bol rahe hain. Itna time to ho gaya, ab to samajh jaana chahiye. Samajh me to aata hai na ki jhooth khya hota hai. Jab tum kehte kuch ho, lekin uska matlab kuch nahi reh jaata. Wo hame kuch bolte hain. AUr hum sun lete hain. Kisi pe vishwas karte hain. Kisi pe nahi. VIshwas kispe karte hain? Jiski baat samajh aaye. Jo lage ki ye karega. Ye sahi bol raha hai. Ye hi layega badlav. Fir kya. VOte dete hain. Fir bakchodi chaalu. Scam. Corruption. Unemlpoyment. Demonetization. 
Is point pe to realize ho hi jaana chahiye ki hamse jhooth bola ja raha hai. Fir bhi har baar uth ke ham vote dene chale jaate hain. Kisi na kisi ko vote deke aa jaate hain. Kyunki hame lagta hai ki iska jhooth best hai. Sabse convincing to yahi bola. Matlab bola to wo bhi ki Education pe paisa lagayega. Crime situation ko better karega. Lekin wo feel nahi aaya. Laga nahi ki wo sahi me karega.  Lekin ye banda jo bola. Wah. Health care pe paisa kharch karega. Wah. Employment layega. Muaah. Wah. YE sabse convincing tha. Mera vote isko.

DOCUMENT: Sketches_and_Monologues/The_Fish_Wife_Curse_Amaze_Balls.md
================================================================================

> [!abstract] Editorial Diagnosis & Triage
> **Verdict:** Story Spine
> **Core Spark:** Subverting the fairy-tale curse resolution: the victim would rather stay a fish than submit to an apology over body-shaming
>
> **Actionable Next Steps:**
> - [x] Harvested fish body vs domestic duties dialogue to Dialogue_and_Voice_Scraps.md
> - [ ] Connected to [[The_God_Hypothesis_Anthology_Spine]] and [[Time_Travelling_Girls_Hawking_Encounter]]
> - [ ] Flesh out The Beast's role as legal/domestic mediator
> - [ ] Introduce the sorcerer's reaction when he realizes his curse failed to punish her

“Look at it” the parrot squawked.
“What is it this time.” said Mr Amaze Balls as he looked up from his scroll. “Oh no. What happened baby?” 
“You mean you know who this is?” the parrot said again. 
“Of course. This is my wife, Gum” Mr Amaze Balls pointed his open hand to the huge fish standing on its tail. Mrs Gum Balls was covered in a rose bush. It looked like she had dragged it for a long distance. “And this is my close adviser and friend, Mr The Beast.” He pointed to the parrot with his thumb. 
Mrs Gum balls tried to speak, but neither of them could understand. “What happened honey”


 “It’s okay honey. Mr Beast is here to help us with your curse.” He then turned his eyes to her tail and asked, “What happened honey. 
Mrs Gum Balls had tears in her eyes. Her tail was bloody. She tried to speak. She gurgled as if there was liquid stuck in her throat. Bubbles came out of her mouth in Mr Balls’ direction. One caught The Beast on his beak. “Don’t worry Mrs Balls, we found this ancient formula to make animals talk like humans.” The bubble was still sitting on top of The Beast’s beak. It made his head look bigger, split in the middle, with four eyes. Mrs Balls gave out a laugh.  More bubbles filled the room. A few more caught The Beast. He opened his wings and shook his tiny body. The bubble on his beak still remained.
Mrs Balls gurgled again looked down and wrapped herself in her gills. “Honey, drink this. This will help you talk.”
Mr Balls walked towards Mrs Balls with a glass filled with a black liquid. He helped her drink the medicine and took a step back. Both The Beast and Mr Amaze Balls looked at Mrs Gum Balls expectantly. She starts coughing. A little at first. Then violently. THe whole room fills with bubbles. Mr Amaze Balls tries to fight the bubbles with the scroll in his hand. The Beast tries to fly to the top of the room to escape them. The room is filled with the sound of coughs, wings fluttering, and an ocassional AARRRRGGHHH from Mr Amaze Balls.
Suddenly the coughing stopped. “I can speak” said a gurgly voice. It sounded like someone’s throat was filled with phlegm. The fluttering stopped.
“That’s great honey. Now tell us what happened.” Said Mr Amaze Balls, as he emerged victorious in his battle. 
“The sorcerer said that he will not take back his curse until I apologize to him.” There were no bubbles this time.
Mr Amaze Balls’ eyes were wide looking at Mrs Balls in anticipation. “Then what?” He said.
“Then… Nothing I came back here, some kids started teasing me on the way and pushed me into a garden. I fell on a rose bush and so…” She waves her gills at her tail. 
“Mrs Balls, I think you misunderstood Amaze. What he meant was, what happened after you apologized.” The beast was now sitting on top of a pile of books. His feathers dripping water all over the books.
“Apologized? Why would I apologize to that freak?”
Mr Amaze Balls and The Beast looked at each other in amazement.
“Because you called him a freak.” Said Mr Amaze Balls “To his face.”
“He called me fat.” Relied Mrs Balls.
“He suggested you swim to exercise.”
“That means fat.”
“Honey, we have scoured all the books that we could find. We’ve looked through all the manuscripts we could steal from library. We even checked on google. There is no other way to cure you. You have to apologize to the sorcerer.” Mr Amaze Balls stretched his hand to the door. He was ordering her now. Mrs Balls put her gills on her hips. Mr Balls was familiar with this look. He took a couple of steps back, “I mean, if you want.” 
“I didn’t marry you so you could make me submit in front of a freak,” gurgled Mrs Balls, “I will not apologize to that dirty little man.”
“Honey don’t you want your old body back.” Mr Balls was pleading with her. “You can’t be a fish all your life.”
“I can’t?” said Mrs Balls, “Who says?”
Mr Balls didn’t know what to say anymore. He looked at the parrot, “The Beast…” He was urging him to say something.
Mr Beast nodded at Mr Amaze Balls. He turned his head to Mrs Balls, “Forgive me Mrs Balls, but do you want to be a fish all your life?”
“Why not.  I have almost zero fat. My eyesight is great. I looove the colors,” Mrs Balls swirled on her tail. The rose bush was still there. “Owwwww”. She untagled some of the branches. “The talking thing was a little annoying but you guys figured it out. So, I love it.” Said Mrs Balls.
“But don’t you want to have a normal life?” said The Beast.
“What normal life?” asked Mrs Balls.
“You know normal things. Cooking, cleaning…” The Beast hesitated. He looked at Mr Amaze Balls who nodded him to go on. “…sex” he said.
“I don’t care really. I love my new fish body. So I’m going to keep it.
